## C-031: The simulated controller: pure and tick-driven, then the whole conversation over a socket
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: a full simulated controller; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": a simulated controller, generalised from Veredas 1's mscsim and trajetorias' automated replies, no vendor named)
- **Description:** `Controller::new(rules, seed)` runs a controller file (C-030) as a pure engine: `tick(elapsed, peers)` advances its clock and returns what is due, `on_message(name, fields, peer)` takes a decoded message and returns what it sends now or later, with a note for the monitor on everything it decides; no socket, no sleep, no wall clock but the one a `now` field asks for, and a seed (the bench's, else the file's) repeating a run exactly, its draws on a stream per purpose (the items, the outcomes, the cyclic messages, the answers), so an item's fields depend on its place alone, whatever a tick carried between two items. It announces an item every interval while a peer is connected and the gate register is not 0, each due an interval after the last was due whatever the tick (a pass announces every item due since the last pass, at most twenty, a longer stall skipping the rest and keeping the phase; when announcing resumes past a due time the first item comes at once), its fields rendered in order (a counter counting, a cycle cycling, a template reading the earlier fields, a range drawn), each fault setting its fields on its share of items, drawn item by item; it awaits the reply keyed by the item's key (trimmed), takes the reply's fields into the item (trimmed), counts a reply for no item and a destination it does not have (the reply's destination field naming none of its destinations, or no number; a reply with no destination field names none; the item still travels), and acts on the first disposition whose field equals: requeue (round again after the lap, its laps counted, its fields kept, held like a new item while no peer is connected or the gate is closed), park (ended, asked about no more), else deliver (travel for the outcome's time, then the outcome to the peer that answered, its fields from the item and the reply, its result forced by the first blocking entry whose bit is set in the destination's word, with the fields it sets and its requeue, else drawn from the mix by weight, else delivered with nothing forced; without an outcome the item ends delivered at once); it answers a message with the answer's fields rendered from the message, to the peer it came from, at once or after the delay, a field that cannot render (no decision, a name not there) refusing the answer, counted; a decision remembers its key while the value is not blocked, takes the first matching rule, draws over the spread avoiding what is blocked, else the reject value, which is never remembered; it obeys a command whose field value matches a setting, setting its registers, and notes one that matches nothing; it sends each cyclic message every interval, peers or none, one per destination with the destination and its word when asked; its status holds the registers, every destination's word, the items awaiting and the age of the oldest, the newest 200 ended items with how each ended, the counters (announced, answered, requeued, parked, unknown answers, invalid destinations, ended by how, answers sent and refused, commands, cyclic sent, faults by name), the interval and each fault's share; the knobs set the interval (0 refused; the next item within the new interval), a fault's share (over 100 or unknown refused), a destination's word, one bit of one or every destination, a register, and announce an item now, each refused by name on what the rules do not have. Riding a bench channel (C-029), the whole conversation runs over a real socket: the machine of the worked example on one end, the unit on the other, the knobs turned live.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/controller.rs | the worked example's machine driven in 10 ms ticks with no sleep: nothing announced without a peer while the heartbeats go out; the first item at once then ten more in a second, their fields exact (the counter, the cycle, the flag, the template, the weight within its range), the status's counts and the oldest unanswered age; the answer for item 1 from peer 5 with a padded key, its note with the latency, the outcome 500 ms later to peer 5 with the item's and the reply's fields and a result from the mix, the history entry with its destination, peer, laps, latency and how it ended; a reply for no item and a destination not in the list each counted and noted, a reply with no destination field counted as nothing with the item travelling to `-`; a message nobody reacts to; the mix drawn by weight (a thousand deliveries 850 to 950 sorted and 50 to 150 lost, the ended counts agreeing; a weight of 0 in front never drawn and the shares 3 to 1 holding over four hundred); requeue bringing the item round after 2000 ms as lap 2 with its fields kept (held while no peer is there, announced the moment one is), park ending it for good, the first matching disposition deciding, none delivering, an item without an outcome ending delivered at once; destination 4 full forcing FULL over the rendered field and requeueing, the bit cleared drawing from the mix, a fault forcing FAULT without a requeue, both bits set deciding by precedence, every chute full and back, every knob's refusal by name, a mix entry that requeues and a mix with no weight ending delivered; ten ticks of 100 ms and one tick of 1000 ms rendering the same eleven weights under one seed, the first item's outcome landing between items in the first and after them in the second; a command stopping the announcements while the heartbeats go on per destination with each word, starting them again at once, setting the speed the status line reads at 5000 ms, one matching nothing and one without the field, the register knob, a cyclic message every 250 ms peers or none with a counter wrapping after its last, the clock, a cycle and a template as sources; the unit answering to the peer at once with the item copied and a destination from the spread, the same code deciding the same twenty times, an empty or blank code taking its rule's 0, the shares over four hundred codes (1 about twice 2 and 3), destination 1 marked full never drawn and a remembered 1 decided afresh, every chute full giving the reject 9 to a read code and a remembered one while an empty code keeps its rule, the chutes back drawing again for a new code and for the one rejected while full, the code remembered before the full period keeping its chute, an answer refused for want of a decision or a name, counted, a delayed answer going out after its delay; the fault's share (about one in ten of a thousand, every fault field set), turned to all and to none, the rate of 100 ms items over 10 s on ticks of 10, 30, 70 and 250 ms alike and of 20 ms items on a 31 ms tick, a stall of ten intervals announcing the ten and one of fifty announcing twenty, the phase kept, one item at once after an idle with no peer, the interval turned live, announce now, two seeds repeating and a third differing, the history capped at 200; then over a real loopback socket, the machine listening and the unit dialling with the channel schema's worked file: items announced, answered within 500 ms, outcomes reported back and read by the unit with their text, heartbeats per destination, the counts of both ends agreeing to within what is in flight, the monitors showing each message's fields, every chute full forcing FULL outcomes and requeues seen by the unit then every chute back, the register stopping the announcements and a command sent by the unit starting them again, noted, the no-read fault turned to all with the unit rejecting to 0, the knobs refused by name and all refused on a channel without a controller, the machine stopped and the unit seeing it go, the port free; the reference's sentences about running quoted beside their pins (C-030's copy holds the text)

## C-032: Controllable pressure: the rate, the faults, every chute full and the no-reads, live on the wire
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: controllable pressure; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": controllable pressure)
- **Description:** The pressure a bench puts on its consumer is turned from the bench channel's handle while the conversation runs, and holds on the wire: the announcement rate is the interval's whatever the host's tick (every item due since the last pass announced, each due an interval after the last was due, so a tick longer than the interval does not cap the rate), changed live by `set_interval` and forced by `announce_now` (one item, no burst); a named fault's share of items is `set_fault`'s, drawn item by item, each faulted item carrying the fault's fields (a no-read's flag and empty code) so the other side treats it as its rules say (the unit rejecting it to destination 0, which the machine counts as an invalid destination); `set_bit_everywhere` marks every chute full, so every outcome at a chute is forced FULL and every item comes round again after the lap, and clears them, so the outcomes are drawn again (an item answered while full may still end FULL once); a peer gone stops the announcements, and the oldest unanswered item ages in the status; a register is set as a command would set it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/pressure.rs | the worked example's machine and unit over loopback, measured on the unit's count of announcements received: 17 to 23 in 2 s at 100 ms, 34 to 46 at 50 ms after `set_interval`, 85 to 115 at 20 ms (below the Windows tick), 4 to 6 at 400 ms, exactly one more on `announce_now` at an interval of a minute and none after; the no-read share at 50 % over a thousand items at 10 ms (44 to 56 by the machine's own count, 35 to 65 among at least 200 announcements the unit showed while the fault was on, gathered by item as its last hundred lines slid, each with its flag and empty code), the unit rejecting to 0 and, once every item is answered, the machine's invalid destinations risen by exactly the no-reads; every chute full: every word's bit set, five more FULL outcomes and requeues within 5 s and the five newest outcomes at a chute the unit shows all FULL, then every chute back with the words cleared, the newest outcomes at a chute drawn again and at most two more FULL from items already answered, an item on its second lap; the unit stopped: the machine's peers gone, nothing more announced over 600 ms and the oldest unanswered item 500 ms older; the register knob
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-032.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-033: The bench file and the executable: one file for the whole plant, run from the command line
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar": a library with an executable on top; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": the bench file, "Rules": a library first, one executable on top; docs/questions.md Q12)
- **Description:** One TOML file stating `format = "veredas-bench"` and `version = 1` (`docs/bench-schema.md`) lists the plant's parts by name: log emitters (C-025), OPC UA servers (C-026), Modbus servers (C-110) and telegram channels (C-029), each channel pointing at the device's own files beside it (core's channel file, the catalog, the controller rules file). `bench::load` reads it and every referenced file and checks each part as its own module checks it, refusing by place another format or version, a misspelled key in any of its thirteen tables, a part without a name or with another part's name, a bad file name or timestamp format, a value range empty or not finite or over 15 decimals, what the OPC UA or the Modbus server refuses, a referenced file that cannot be read or does not load, an id field not riding in the framing, a rules file not loading against the catalog, a message the catalog lacks or whose fields leave a name unwritten or use a source a plain channel lacks, before anything binds, opens or spawns; `Bench::start` runs every part behind one handle, emitters first, then servers (OPC UA, then Modbus), then channels, each kind in the file's order (TOML keeps no order across kinds; an emitter without a seed drawing from the bench's seed plus its index, each channel and its controller from the bench's seed, the output folder created), a part that cannot start stopping the ones before it, every one of them joined, and named, with every part's status by name in that order, one line per part, a channel or a server by name for the knobs, and stop ending every part. The executable `veredas-maquinar <bench.toml>` runs a bench file until stdin closes or says `stop`, printing every part's line at the start (a server's with the address it got) and once a second, exit 0; `--check` reads, checks and prints the plan without starting anything; a refused file or a part that cannot start is exit 2 with the reason on stderr; a usage error exit 1. The worked example loads, starts with every part doing its work and runs under the executable; every key statement of the reference is the reader's, and the test quotes each statement beside its pin and holds the reference's text (the Verification). A desktop UI was Q12; the architect decided (2026-11-07) that the library and this executable are the bench.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/bench_file.rs | the worked example's folder written from the references (the channel file, the catalog, the two rules files, the bench file with a free port): loaded with the prose's numbers (the line interval, the rotation, the draw's range and interval, the step's delay, the port the reference names) and its four-line plan exact; started: the emitter's lines in a daily file under `logs` with codes and weights in their ranges and no error, the server advertising a chosen port, drawing twice within 5 s, a speed within its range read back and a reaction fired by a set, the machine with its peer answering three items and the unit's own status line received, one status line per part in the file's order with their words, unknown names none, stopped with the port free and the file whole; the reference's thirteen tables' key lists equal to the reader's own (from a misspelled key placed in each through `load`), each key left out refused where required (the header's two with their own words) and loading with the stated default otherwise (the top level's parts as sections, a server's tags with its reactions), the type, access, encoding and role words the reader's own variant lists; the header refused four ways with the body judged after it, the emptiest bench loading and running with no part; every refusal of the closing paragraph with its exact message (a nameless and a padded name, a name taken across kinds, an empty and an unwritable file pattern, a bad timestamp format, a nameless value, an empty and an infinite range, decimals over 15, the server's own refusal and its empty bind each with the part named, an empty host, a channel file missing or of another format with its path, a catalog whose id field does not ride, a rules file naming a message the catalog lacks with its path, a message the catalog lacks, a name unwritten, `from` in a plain channel); a server on a taken port refused at start by name with the emitter started before it stopped and joined before the error came back (its file's state, a first line or none yet when the bind failed before the thread's first tick as a loaded runner showed, unchanged over two and a half of its intervals), a server started before a failing one with its port free the moment the error is back (joined, not told), a channel listed after a failing server never dialling the socket it would have dialled, a folder that cannot be created refused by place; a file listing a server above an emitter above a channel started, shown and stopped as emitter, server, channel; the same bench twice with two emitters without seeds writing the same lines run for run and different lines from each other, the machine's first two items, read once both stand in the status (announced, and no longer travelling), drawn alike for one bench seed and differently for another, a channel's own drawn field, four lines kept by a peer, alike for one bench seed and differently for another; the executable: `--check` printing the four-part plan with nothing bound or written, a refused file exit 2 with the place on stderr, a missing file exit 2, no file and an unknown option exit 1 with the usage, a run printing the parts' lines with the emitter writing, the machine announcing and the unit connected, stopped by `stop` on stdin with exit 0 and `stopped` last, the port free and the log file there, and a second run stopped by stdin closing alike; the reference's text equal, paragraph by paragraph, to the copy the test holds

- **Description:** `crates/veredas-portal` is the one point where the suite exchanges data with the outside world (the plan's scope; `docs/portal-plan.md` maps what it absorbs from Veredas 1's `veredas-core` (the data plane), `veredas-store`, `veredas-server` and `veredas-desktop`, what it leaves there, and the phases: the data plane, telegram channels by rules, OPC sources, the session store, the server, the project file with P-010, the shell as `docs/questions.md` Q14). ## C-035: The file tail: one growing file followed through truncation and replacement, text decoded whatever its encoding
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: the data plane, tailing; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** A `FileTailSource` follows one file at a path `tail -F` style on its own thread, poll-driven (a filesystem watcher wakes the poll early on a local path; a share is polled alone, a UNC path or a mapped network drive on Windows, while a mount on Linux reads as local until P-013), emitting every completed line as a `LineEvent` tagged with its source, a dense per-source sequence from 0, the arrival clock, and the stamp a `TimestampSpec` reads from the line (none when it does not parse; the line still flows), a line's time index being the parsed stamp read as UTC, else its arrival: CRLF stripped, a bare carriage return kept, an empty line a line, a partial line held until its newline; started at the end it skips what exists and the torn fragment a mid-line end leaves, started at the beginning it reads the file whole; a file absent at attach is read whole when it appears; an in-place truncation (the file seen shorter than what was read; a rewrite back at or past the read offset by the next poll is read as a continuation, the limit of every poll-based tail) flushes the held partial and rereads from the top; a replacement or a deletion drains the old file to a stable end (its torn last line flushed, never lost; one found shorter than what was read, truncated then replaced between two looks, reread from its top to its stable end first) and follows whatever appears at the path from its start; a stop flushes the final partial and the worker ends cleanly; a writer faster than the reader never pins the reader and loses no line; a tail no watcher serves (the folder not there yet, the watcher off or unobtainable) polls at its interval, never spinning, and reads the file when it comes; a start returns once the worker has looked at its path (present, attached where the start says; present but not openable for the moment, a writer holding it or a share's passing error, awaited: when it opens, the same file (its identity, a length not shrunk) is read from where it stood at the look, so what was written to it meanwhile is read and its history stays history, and a file replaced meanwhile is read whole, as is one whose identity the look could not read, denied even its attributes, or one the look could read nothing of, not even its length (its folder denying the listing, a share's passing error), duplicates over loss; absent, awaited and read whole when it appears; within five seconds at most, the watcher armed after the signal so its arming never eats the bound), so a line written after the start is never taken as history when the look completes within the bound. Text is decoded as UTF-8 (a byte-order mark or none), UTF-16 in either order (a mark, or the zero pattern of ASCII-heavy text without one) or CP1252 (the fallback), decided by the first bytes that tell (a mark or the UTF-16 pattern in the first bytes, else the first byte beyond ASCII, so a file that opens with plain ASCII lines streams them and is not committed before its first accented character, however far in; a tail attached at the end reads the file's head to decide, tells a UTF-16 line feed from a torn line, takes a file holding its mark alone as a clean start, and moves an attach that falls inside a UTF-16 code unit back to the unit's boundary, dropping the fragment it skips before the decoder sees it while the head left the encoding undecided, so an attach inside a UTF-8 sequence of an ASCII-headed file never commits CP1252; a head under four bytes that is no complete mark is too little to decide from, and its bytes vote with what follows them as a start from the beginning does, a torn mark being no text yet and a clean start, and what it held being text from before the attach unless it proves a mark, skipped with the torn line it opens; a NUL beside a line feed in the first bytes waits for the UTF-16 pattern rather than decide on the line feed) and committed for the file's lifetime, a multi-byte sequence or code unit split across appends decoded whole, a torn final byte (one that starts a UTF-8 sequence the stream ends before completing) a replacement character, a byte no sequence starts the CP1252 character it is. Reading never blocks the plant's writer: every handle is a plain open with full sharing, never a memory map, so the owner appends, truncates, renames and deletes while the tail reads. A `SourceSet` merges every source's stream into one receiver, tagged and sequenced per source, that disconnects only after every source is stopped; a source is removed alone, a worker's liveness and failure are reported without blocking, a dropped consumer ends a worker cleanly, and a stop reaches a busy worker.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/tail_file.rs | over real files in a scratch folder: appends in order with the sequence, the source, the arrival bracketed by the clock and the index equal to the arrival; CRLF stripped, a bare CR kept, an empty line; a partial held then completed; a start at the end skipping history and the torn fragment, and emitting the next line of a file ending in a newline; truncation flushing the partial and rereading; a replacement draining the torn last line then reading the new file; a deleted file drained and the reappearing one read from its start; a late file read whole; a stop flushing the final partial with the worker's clean result; 20 000 lines from a flat-out writer arriving once each in order; UTF-8 with and without a mark, UTF-16 in both orders with a mark and little-endian without, CP1252, a UTF-8 sequence and a UTF-16 code unit split across appends, a torn final byte as U+FFFD; a timestamp rule stamping two lines and leaving a continuation line unstamped with its sequence and its arrival index; the owner appending three times by open-append-close, truncating, renaming and deleting while a running tail reads, every owner operation succeeding and every line the owner wrote reaching the tail; UNC paths remote and a drive local (on Windows, where those shapes exist), a relative path classified without a panic; the merged stream of two list sources tagged and sequenced, the receiver connected until stop-all and disconnected after, a stop reaching an endless worker and a dropped consumer ending one, remove stopping one of two with the other still producing, the reports of a live, an ended and a failed worker with the failure's exact text kept across remove, and three files tailed at once with 900 lines merged, each tagged by content and dense per source; a UTF-16 file in either order tailed from its end, ending on a line feed (the first new line emitted) and mid-line (the torn line's completion skipped); an empty file then a header then CP1252 decoded as CP1252 (and committed), an ASCII opening then UTF-8 decoded whole, a tail attached at the end of an ASCII file decoding the CP1252 line appended; three tails no watcher serves (a parent folder not there yet, the watcher off, the watcher asked for on a missing folder) costing under 1.5 s of the process's CPU over 3 s and each reading its file when the folder and the files come; a file holding a UTF-16 (either order) or a UTF-8 mark alone, tailed from its end, emitting the two lines then written; a UTF-16 file in either order cut one byte into a code unit, inside a line (the torn line's completion skipped, the next two whole lines first) and at a line's start (that line whole, then the next); an ASCII-opened file ending on a torn UTF-8 lead flushed as U+FFFD at the stop, and on a byte no sequence starts as its CP1252 character; an ASCII head over 4 KiB ending inside a two-byte UTF-8 character, tailed from its end, the continuation byte then two accented lines appended, both decoded as UTF-8; a file holding one byte of a UTF-16 mark in either order, or two of the UTF-8 mark, tailed from its end and the rest appended, the two lines then written whole; one UTF-16 character without a mark, in either order, its line skipped and the next two whole; a CP1252 thorn and a UTF-8 character opening like a mark, held at the attach, never emitted; a UTF-16 file without a mark opening on a bare line feed, UTF-16 from its end and from its beginning alike; a short head the stop reaches undecided emitting nothing; thirty tails started at the end of a file and appended to right after the start returned, each reading the line; a file absent at the start, created with a line right after, read whole; a file held against every open at the start and released after, started at the end, emitting the line appended then and none of its history (where a lock can be held); a file held at the start and appended to during the hold emitting the line appended during the hold and the one after, none of its history; a file replaced by a rename during the hold read whole, with the line appended after; a file denied to its reader at the start, no identity readable, replaced by a rename and the replacement read whole with the line appended after (where an access-control list can deny, Windows); a file unreadable by its mode at the start and released, the line appended then read and none of its history, and one replaced by a rename during the hold read whole (Unix, not as root); a file truncated to a torn line, renamed away and replaced by a new file between two polls (the watcher off), the torn line then the new file's line; a file denied to its reader in a folder denied its listing at the start, replaced by a rename, the replacement read whole with the line appended after (Windows); a file in a folder denying search at the start, the folder opened again and the file replaced before the next look, the replacement read whole with the line appended after (Unix, not as root)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-035.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-036: Log families: a rotating family followed through its rotations with no line lost or duplicated
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: log families; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope"; proven against the bench's emitters, C-025)
- **Description:** A `FamilySpec` is a regex over file names, matched whole (a relative `/`-separated path when recursive, bounded in depth, a folder of a member's name no member), with two reserved groups: `date`, parsed with its chrono format (a date alone midnight; a year alone its first day, a month its first, an hour without its minutes the start of the hour, twelve-hour or twenty-four, padded or not; a token that fails its parse degrades to none and the file stays a member), and `idx`, parsed as a number (so `_10` follows `_2`); `resolve` lists the members in rotation order (the key, then the modification time, then the name, a keyless member first) with the active file last and marked: the newest by key in `NewestByOrder`, the file of the constant name in `Fixed` (absent, no file is active); a date group without its format or a format without the group, and a bad pattern, are refused by name; an unreadable folder is an I/O error naming it. A `FamilyTailSource` follows the family on its own thread through its rotations with zero lines lost, zero duplicated and the order kept, whatever the writer does: a clean rotation, a straggler line landing late in the retired file (drained to a stable end before the handoff), torn split writes, generations born inside one rescan (every generation between the one being read and the newest consumed, oldest first, before the newest is read), a fixed-name active renamed into the pattern (recognised by identity, never by position, so the file just opened is never read twice; the fixed path checked again after every listing, so a burst of rotations between two looks is never taken for members dropped in from outside and lost; a sweep that meets the file the cursor just opened, retired while the sweep ran, ends there, so a generation that retired after it waits for the cursor's own lines and the order holds through a second rotation inside the sweep); a family born after attach is all new content, every birth-window generation included, in both modes; a family that predates the session is followed from its active file alone, from its beginning or its end as configured, its retirees history; a family's start returns once its folder has been listed (or found absent) or its backfill located, so a line written right after it is never history, and a fixed-name active not yet born at the start is followed as a family born after the start, the retirees listed then being the history, so a generation that retires before the active is opened is read, never history; a family is followed by its keys: a pattern with neither a `date` nor an `idx` group is listed (by modification time, then name) but refused for following, by text, a pre-existing family on which no key parses is refused by text, and members whose keys tie are followed by name, the lower name history (the listing breaks a tie by time), so the keys must separate generations and a writer that reuses one is not followed. The bench's emitter (C-025) writes the files a plant writes, rotating on a line count into dated, indexed names, in UTF-8 or UTF-16 with its mark; its `family_shape` is the spec that resolves them, in rotation order with the date read back (a year alone as its first day, a month as its first, an hour without its minutes as the start of the hour; a pattern whose date is not one run of specifiers carrying a year has no shape), and the tail attached before the first file emits exactly the lines on disk, in order, once.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/tail_family.rs | resolving: unpadded indices numerically with the newest active and the key read, dated names chronologically and date-plus-index by both, a full date-time token and a date alone, fixed mode with the active last and marked and without it, non-members and a whole-name match and a folder of a member's name excluded, an unparseable date token first with a `NONE` key, a recursive scan with relative names ordered across subfolders and the flat spec seeing the flat folder, a pattern confined to a subtree, the same name in two folders kept apart, both spec refusals with their exact text and a bad pattern, keyless members ordered deterministically, a missing folder as an I/O error naming it; ## C-038: Capture rules: a line read into typed series by a regex's named groups, the stamp parsed by ordered formats
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: regex capture; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** A `CaptureRule` (a name, a `regex` pattern with named groups, a scope of one source or every source, a type per group, an optional key group) turns a matching `LineEvent` into one `Sample` per named group with a value, every sample carrying the source, the series (the group's name), the key captured from the key group (none when absent), the line's stamp, its sequence and its arrival; a group is text unless typed int (decimal, trimmed), float (what the parser takes, trimmed) or time (the source's chrono formats in order, a date alone midnight), and a value that refuses its type is counted and never kills the line; the key group is no series; an optional group absent on the line emits nothing and counts nothing; the key is carried even when every value failed; a line outside the scope or not matching yields nothing. Construction refuses a bad pattern (lookaround included: one engine, `regex` syntax alone, as C-013 reads a message body by rules), a pattern with no named groups, and a key group the pattern lacks, naming the rule. `named_groups` lists a pattern's named groups in order, none for a bad or groupless pattern. A `TimestampSpec` locates a line's stamp by a pattern (its `ts` group, else the whole match) and parses it with ordered chrono formats (a fraction, a date alone as midnight), refusing an empty format list and a bad pattern; a `TimestampExtractor` keeps the format that last succeeded preferred (tried first; when it misses, the configured order with it skipped, never a rotation from it, so a format that never succeeded is not preferred over an earlier one), so an ambiguous stamp resolves the same way for a whole stream, while the stateless spec tries the configured order every time.

## C-039: A portal telegram channel: a device by its channel file, its catalog and reply rules, every message a typed row, every reply gated
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", rules: a device spoken over TCP is a telegram channel configured by encoding and decoding rules, its data stored like any other protocol's; docs/questions.md Q8; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope"; proven against the bench channel, C-029, its controller, C-031, and its pressure, C-032)
- **Description:** `channel::spawn` runs one TCP link to a device on its own thread, in either role, configured by core's channel file (C-017) and catalog (C-028) and by the gateway's reply rules, never by code: before anything binds or dials it asks the catalog whether its ids ride the framing (`Catalog::check_framing`, refused with the catalog's reason), compiles every reply rule against the catalog (the messages named must exist, every name the sent message writes given exactly once by a constant or by a field the answered message reads as the catalog says, a field with no source or two refused) and shapes every message's table (one column per binary field, typed by the catalog, an integer field an `Int`, text and hex `Text`; the payload's groups and a repeat's numbered names `Text`, a repeat wider than the table cap refused), each refusal naming the channel, the rule and the place. Running, it reads every frame's messages by the catalog's placing (`messages_of`, `decode`) and emits each as an event carrying the peer, the plant-local index clock, the unix arrival clock, the message's name and id, its fields and the raw frame, which decodes again; an id the catalog lacks or a body its rules refuse is an `Unreadable` event, counted apart; a keep-alive is reported; the link's state (waiting, linked with its peers, trouble with the wire's reason, stopped) is reported as it changes; a `MessageTable::row` turns the fields into a row positional against the columns, a missing name or a non-number in an integer column a hole. A reply rule met by its message composes the reply by the catalog (`encode`, the body placed by `frame`) and sends it to the peer the message came from, only while the master write switch (core's `WriteGate`, shared with every write path of the session) is on: off, the reply is withheld and counted, nothing composed, nothing sent, the device waiting on every item, and arming the switch answers the next message, live; a reply that cannot render or that the catalog refuses is refused and counted, never sent. The handle's `send` is the second write path of the same gate: the switch first (`Disarmed`, nothing composed), then a message the catalog lacks, a value missing or not fitting, an empty body in a layout without segments, a frame the framing refuses, a peer not connected or no peer, each a typed refusal with nothing on the wire; a message the catalog writes reaches the device by name, to one peer or to all. The status counts messages received and sent by name, unknown ids, undecodable bodies, replies sent, withheld and refused, and keeps the last refusal's text, a disarmed send's included; a peer gone between the look and the write, within one tick, is the wire's own error, the send counted; a stop is prompt and frees the port. Its reply rules are the gateway's own, not a controller file (C-030): the same act as a controller file's `[[answers]]` (a message answered by another sent back to its peer, every name it writes given), narrowed to a constant or a copied field and gated by the switch, each side checking its names through the catalog's `names` and `reads` (C-028).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/channel.rs | the controller reference's catalog: the announcement's and the outcome's tables typed column by column, a row from fields, a missing name and a non-number as holes, a repeat's numbered columns after a payload group, the cap refused one over and accepted at it. Against the bench's machine, started from a bench file in a scratch folder over loopback: armed, the link waiting then linked, at least five announcements as rows exact to the rules file (the counter from 1, the lane cycling, the flag and the template or the no-read fault's flag and empty code, the weight in range), every one answered by the rule and every outcome back to destination 1 with a result of the mix, heartbeats for the four destinations with destination 4's full bit, every message's arrival within the clock and its index offset by the local zone, every raw frame decoding whole, the machine's own count agreeing (answered at least five, no unknown answer, no invalid destination), the status's counts exact, a stop within 1.5 s seen by the machine as its peer gone; disarmed, at least five announcements read and every reply withheld with no outcome, the machine answered nothing and waiting on five, the wire having sent the connect keep-alive alone, then the switch armed live and the next item answered with its outcome, then disarmed again and nothing more answered; the handle's send disarmed refused with the machine's register untouched, an unknown message, a missing value and a value too wide refused by the catalog's text with no command counted, an unknown peer refused, a stop command obeyed (the register 0, the announcements stopped, one command counted) and a command to one peer by id setting the speed, the last refusal's text kept, a send after the stop reporting the worker gone; the no-read fault at 100 % showing in every following row and a 20 ms interval giving at least 30 rows a second; a plant catalog with an extra id and a longer command: one unknown id and one refused body, each with its bytes, beside a status line read, the counts exact; a listener waiting with its bind in the wire's state, linked when the machine dials, waiting again when the machine stops, linked by a second plant, the port free after the stop; a dialler with nobody there in trouble with the connect error, linked when the plant appears; every refusal before anything binds with its exact value (the catalog's id field absent from the framing and one with a role, each of eight bad rules at its index, a repeat of a thousand) and the port the refused channels would have dialled never dialled, while a rule reading the outcome's payload groups is accepted; with nobody connected and no bench: a send refused for a peer then a disarmed one, the last refusal's text the switch's; an unknown message and a missing value refused by the catalog before the peer is asked for, a good command refused for no peer and for an unknown peer

The table surface lists every table and view with its columns (an exact row count for a table, none for a view; a view that cannot be described now, a linked file gone between a removal and its replacement, listed with no columns and hiding no other table), pages a table's rows newest first by its time column, every other column breaking a tie so no row repeats or is skipped across pages (two rows identical in every cell aside), with every cell typed as JSON (a timestamp, a date and a time of day as milliseconds, a decimal a number, an interval an ISO 8601 duration with its sign on the seconds, a blob hex, a hole null, a nested value alone as the engine's debug text), windowed, offset and bounded by the page cap, and charts one numeric column of a table or view keyed or not (a key asked of a table with no key column refused as a name the catalog lacks), every name checked against the catalog so no text reaches the SQL unquoted; a snapshot writes a standalone database file any tool reads, tables as data and views as views, never over an existing file (refused by name before anything is attached; a refused snapshot leaves nothing attached, and the next one writes); a table or view exports to CSV with a header and the whole database to a folder with its schema; a CSV the operator keeps is read live through a view (its edits, a replacement and a new column seen on the next query, the file never held open), the engine's sniffer its first reader, the padded reader taking any file the sniffer would read ragged (a collapsed delimiter, a short row, a long row: any outcome but a regular table, read off the file itself over the first 2 048 lines in the sniffer's delimiter with quotes honoured), so every row is kept, a short one padded, a long one widening the schema within those lines and the read refused by its line past them (`docs/questions.md` Q17), a decimal comma adopted only where reading so strictly improves the typing, and previewed with no session. `COPY FROM DATABASE` snapshots tables as data and views as views into a file a fresh connection reads; a file-backed database takes a memory limit and a temp directory and reopens with its rows; a connection behind a mutex is shared across threads; a view over `read_csv_auto` re-reads the file on every query, never holds it open, and re-expands its columns; and the engine reports the pinned version; and, the read side the store relies on: the value variants the engine hands back for a date, a time, a decimal, an interval, a huge integer and a timestamp; the listing's aggregate (a count, a distinct count filtered, a max over a case, an `arg_max`) over a union skipping null cells and typing the last value; a positional tie-break after the time column ordering every column with nulls last under `DESC`; an empty table copied to a header alone; `EXPORT DATABASE` writing the schema with its views and one file per table under a sanitised name inside the folder; the CSV reader's options typing a decimal comma and, on a ragged file, padding a short row, widening the schema by a numbered column for a long row within the first 2 048 lines (the header included) and refusing the read with the line named for one past them, whatever the sample size; a view binding when it is created (a reference to a table not there yet refused with the catalog's text and no view left behind, a bad column with the binder's text, the retry succeeding once the reference exists), which the store's fixpoint over view definitions rests on; the appender taking the store's typed cells (a text, a null, a microsecond timestamp, a double, nulls in typed columns) through a slice of `ToSql` and reading them back exactly; the sniffer's outcomes on a marked, a header-only, an empty and a missing file, and on a ragged file (a short row collapsing it to one column; a long row on any line up to 2 047 with a short row after it collapsing the file to one column, numeric or text alike, whatever the file's length; a run of long rows ending the file and starting on line 2 048 or before kept alone of all its rows (runs on lines 2 047 to 2 049 and 2 048 to 2 050 kept whole), its first row read as the header, duplicate names made unique, when every column the run gives is text or one of its cells cannot be read as its column's type (the types from the rows after it, or from the row itself when it is alone), and kept as data otherwise (as probed: a run of numeric rows or of rows mixing text and numbers kept whole under engine-named columns, a single mixed row kept, a run of all-text rows or a numeric row before a text one losing its first row to the header, a single all-text row keeping none); a long row on line 2 048 with a short row after it, a single long row ending the file on line 2 049, and long rows from line 2 049 up to line 20 480, the engine's default sample size, refusing the sniff; one past line 20 480 refusing the read with the line named); a copy into a foreign database refused by the engine on the first table it holds and detached after.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/store_engine.rs | a thousand appended rows counted by a cloned connection on another thread; a value past 2^62 and a microsecond timestamp read back equal and the timestamp's `epoch_ms`; the `ASOF` pairs exact with the out-of-tolerance one filtered; three bucket anchors exact; `DESCRIBE` columns exact, a cast the run refuses described while the run errors, and the binder's exact words for the bad column; the one table, the one view and the three columns in order; a positive memory figure and a non-empty plan; a snapshot's rows and view read by a fresh connection; the limit's exact text and the temp directory read back and 100 rows reopened; a query through the shared mutex on another thread; the CSV view's count after an edit, a value changed, a count after a replacement by rename, a fourth column seen in the describe and in the row; the engine's version text; the six value variants exact; the aggregate over a union (2, 2, 2 and the last as a double, a microsecond timestamp and a text); the positional order over four rows with a null; the header alone of an empty table; the export folder's six exact file names and the view in its schema; the decimal comma typed double and read as 1.5 beside the sniffer's text, two ragged rows padded, a long row in the sample giving a fourth column and three rows kept, a long row at line 30002 refusing the read by that line, a long row at line 2048 widening and one at 2049 refusing by that line, the whole file sampled refusing at 30002 all the same; a forward view reference refused with the catalog's text and no view left, a bad column refused with the binder's text, the retry making two views; the seven typed cells appended through a slice and read back equal; the filtered distinct count 2 against the plain count 3 on a keyed row with a null value; the marked file typed, the header alone typed text with no rows, the empty file one text column, the missing file's error text; the ragged files: one column and three rows for a short row, four columns and one row for a long row at line 2048, the sniff refused for one at 2049, three columns and the read refused at line 20481 for one there, the sniff refused for one at 20480; the kept row (9, 9, 9, 9) named, a text long row last keeping no row, a long first data row collapsing to one column with its three lines, a numeric and a text long row with rows after them collapsing with their lines, a long row at line 2048 with a row after it refusing the sniff, one at line 2047 with a row after it collapsing with every row kept, one at line 1002 of a 3000-line file collapsing with every row kept, a trailing run of two long rows kept whole, runs on lines 2047 to 2049 and 2048 to 2050 kept whole with their fourth cells, a trailing run of two text long rows naming the four text columns by its first and keeping its second, a run of two mixed rows and a single mixed row kept whole under engine-named columns, a numeric row before a text one taken as the header with its names made unique, a numeric row before a mixed one kept as data with both rows, two identical all-text rows losing the first to the header; ## C-043: The session: a project's sources of the three protocols running as workers, every row into one store alike, the ingest keeping what a shell and the server read
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal": the gateway runs the project; docs/portal-plan.md "Module map": session)
- **Description:** A `Session` starts from a `SessionConfig` (the one a project file yields, `Project::session_config`, C-046): the log sources (a file or a family, each with an optional timestamp rule), the capture rules (each a wide base table named for the rule, its typed groups the columns, its key group the key), the OPC sources (compiled as C-040, each rule a table), the telegram channels (each catalog message a table named for the message, with C-041's rows) and the project's SQL views. Everything is checked before anything spawns (the channels whole, through the channel's own check: the framing, the reply rules, the tables), and refused by name: no source, an empty or a duplicate source id, a rule's scope naming no log source, an unknown group type, a rule or group name outside the name shape of C-040, a table name taken twice across rules, OPC rules, messages and views, whatever the case (naming both owners), a start point in the past into a family without a timestamp rule (nothing to locate it by), and the OPC and channel compile refusals (C-040, C-039). Started, every worker runs on its own thread over the store's (C-041) base tables; a start point in the past reads a file whole and backfills a family (C-037), none follows every log live from its end. A pump thread of its own drains every source's events into wide rows (a captured line, a mapped poll, a read message), flushes them into the store and keeps what a reader needs without a store scan: every series' type, count, distinct keys (capped at 64, flagged), last index and last value; the store's vitals (value cells, bytes, type conflicts); the ingest's counters (lines, polls, messages, rows, failures, store errors, the last error's text); and every source's health on the arrival clock (waiting before anything arrived, live within the idle window, idle past it with the silence, retrying with the reason while an OPC server or a peer is away, ended, or failed with the worker's text), refreshed every 500 ms. A pass never waits on a reader: the store's memory figure among the vitals is read through the ingest's own connection, so a reader holding the store's query connection (a heavy window, an export) delays no row. It broadcasts every numeric sample as JSON data frames to whoever listens, each sample with its source, series, key, stamp, value and the series' count with it (the count the cache shows once it holds that sample, so a listener orders two samples under one stamp); a slow listener drops frames, nothing backpressures the plant. One master write switch, off unless the project says so, is the gate every OPC handle and channel handle of the session shares: set live, the next write obeys it, and a tag no rule marks writable is refused whole, armed or not. A stop ends every worker first, then the pump, whose last passes drain the channels dry (every row the workers sent before the stop stored, cap or no cap) and publish a last refresh, every source ended or failed with its text and the counters final, joins them, and reports each worker's end.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/session.rs | against the bench's plant (its OPC UA server and the controller reference's machine over real sockets) and a real log: a session over the three with a start point in the past and a view: the sources listed by kind, 20 stamped lines captured as typed rows keyed by the item and indexed on the stamp (the first and the last exact, 7 for one key), the view's 9 rows, the server's polls as rows with the drawn speed in its bounds and the word as configured, the machine's announcement in its message's table with its source and its integer item, the series cache listing the three sources with the exact type, count, keys, last index and last value, every source's health live with the log's count and last stamp, the vitals and the counters, ten lines appended (a line no rule reads among them) arriving as frames with their exact stamps, values, keys and the series' counts (21 to 30) and as rows, the text state's count 20 with its samples though no frame carries them, a stop ending the three workers cleanly and the machine seeing its peer go, then every numeric series of the log and the server framed (a float and the integers alike), each series framed counting one up from frame to frame to the cache's count and a keyless sample framed with no key (the machine announces before anyone listens, so its item is checked through the cache, not a frame), the cache's type, count, keys, last index and last value for the log's temperature, the server's speed and word and the machine's item as the store holds them, and the state's count 30; the refusals' exact texts (no source, a duplicate id, a blank id, an unknown scope, an unknown type, a table name taken by a rule and a message, a backfill without a timestamp rule); the switch off by default refusing an OPC write and a channel send before anything reaches the plant (the word unchanged, the machine running), armed live writing the word (seen on the server) and stopping the machine by the command, a polled tag not marked writable refused whole, disarmed live refusing both again with the word kept; a quiet log read as waiting, then live after a line with its count, then idle with its silence past the window, the last stamp kept; a rule name outside the shape, a view named like a base table and two views of one name refused with their exact texts; a 60 000-line file read whole with the pump held on its refresh while the stop begins, every row stored and counted and the source ended after the stop; a family on which no key parses failed at once with its text, the stop returning the failure and the health keeping it after; seventy keys on one series listed as 64 and capped, the server and the peer away before the start read as retrying with the reason, a listener that never reads lagged once more than 256 frames went out while every line kept reaching the store (the server and the peer dialled on ports held bound and listening for nobody, never ports a stopped bench freed); an export of the rows into a named pipe nobody reads holding the store's query connection while five lines are written: the pump passing at least ten times more and the lines and rows at 45 before the pipe is read, the export then ending with a header and the forty rows it saw (Unix, where the pipe is a FIFO)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-043.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-044: The server: the live stream and windowed REST over a session, the write surface refused by default on the wire and whitelisted
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal": serves the data and gates the writes; docs/portal-plan.md "Module map": serve; Veredas 1's server as it was)
- **Description:** Under the `serve` feature, a `router` over one session's `Live` (C-043), served on a bound listener. `GET /events` is a server-sent event stream opening with a `hello` event carrying the session snapshot, then every data frame the ingest broadcasts as a `data` event, a `lag` event carrying the count of frames a slow client missed (its cue to re-window; the stream goes on, nothing lost unsaid), a keep-alive comment every five seconds holding an idle connection. `GET /api/session` is the snapshot from the ingest's caches, never a store query: the session's name, start and started, whether it still runs (false once its stop has published the end), the store axis now and the arrival now, the sources by kind, every source's health as a label with its detail, count, last arrival and last stamp, the vitals, the counters, the switch's position. `GET /api/series` is the listing from the cache, typed and counted, sorted by source then series. `GET /api/series/data` is a numeric window of one series (`from` and `to` inclusive, an optional `key`) downsampled server-side by largest-triangle-three-buckets to a point budget clamped to 10..=5000 (1000 by default), the endpoints always kept, with the raw total and whether it was downsampled. `GET /api/tables` is the store's catalog with kinds, columns and exact row counts; `GET /api/table/data` a windowed page of typed rows with its schema, total and truncation, or with `y` one column charted like a series window; an unknown table is 404 with its name. The write surface is a whitelist over the handles' own gates (C-040, C-039), never a passthrough: `GET /api/opc/read` reads one polled tag on demand (no switch needed; 404 for a tag not in the session); `POST /api/opc/write` and `POST /api/channels/send` answer 403 with the gate's text while the switch is off, before a byte of the body is read; armed, a body that is not the shape is 400 in the same error form, one over 64 KiB 413 in it (a tag's value or a message's fields never fill that), a tag no rule marks writable, an unknown OPC source or an unknown channel is 403 by name, a value that does not fit the tag's type 400 by name, an unknown message or a body the catalog refuses 400, a write the plant never saw 503 (a server away; a peer not there, or a channel's worker gone or silent, a channel's wire carrying no confirmation of its own), an OPC write it may have seen 504. The switch is armed in process alone: no endpoint sets it. Every query is a pure function of its parameters over the store, so the server holds no state per client.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/serve.rs | over a real socket with a raw TCP client, against the bench's plant: the snapshot's running flag, name, start, the two clocks, the sources by kind, the switch off, every source's health live with the log's count and last stamp, the vitals and the counters; the listing with the exact type, count, keys and last index of a series and its sort order; a window of 40 points downsampled to 10 with the exact endpoints, a window of 10 inside it passed through whole, a key narrowing it to 13; the catalog with the base tables, the samples table and the view, a table's exact row count and columns; a page of 5 rows of 40 truncated, the view's 19, a column charted to 10 points, an unknown table's 404 text; a tag read on demand with its value, and an unknown tag's 404 text; the write surface: an OPC write and a channel send refused 403 by default with the gate's text and nothing at the plant (the word unchanged, the machine running, no command counted), the switch armed in process shown by the snapshot, a polled tag not writable and an unknown source refused 403 by name, a value that does not fit 400 by name, the write landing on the server, an unknown message and a missing value refused 400 by the catalog, the command stopping the machine, the switch off again refusing both with the word kept; the stream's hello (a 200, the event-stream type, the running snapshot by name) then five lines appended arriving as data events whose samples carry the exact stamps and values; the budget 1000 by default and clamped to 10 and 5000 (purely) and three points asked giving ten over the socket, the 504 of a write unconfirmed (purely); disarmed, a value as a number, a send without a message, a body that is no JSON and a 3 MiB body to each route all 403 with the switch's text; armed, the two shapes 400 in the error form, a 3 MiB body 413 in it with the limit named, an unknown channel 403 by name, a peer not there 503 by text; a client that never reads, four hundred large frames pushed with the plant stopped and nothing else flowing: the frames it receives and the counts its lag events carry summing to four hundred, every lag event carrying its count, the frame sent after them read; the server stopped, a fresh stream's hello then two comments within fourteen seconds, the first four to seven seconds after the hello and the next four to seven after it, a write 503 by text; the session stopped, the snapshot not running and the log ended
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-044.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-045: One header reader in core: `format` and `version` read before the body, refused by name, or handed to a migration
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files"; P-010: "the header reader is written out in each, ten lines, until a second component needs core's")
- **Description:** `veredas_core::schema` reads the stamp every plain-text file of the suite opens with, before the body: `check(text, noun, format, version)` for a reader that knows one version, refusing in this order and in these words (the noun the reader's own, as `a catalog file`): not TOML with the parser's reason, no `format`, a `format` that is not a string (`` not a catalog file: `format` is not a string ``: a stamp of the wrong type is no stamp), another format (`` `format` is `x`, not `y` ``), no `version`, a `version` that is not a whole number from 0 to 4294967295 (named the same way), another version (`version N of `x` is not known here (this reader knows M)`); the format is decided whole before the version is looked at, so a file of another format is refused for its format whatever its version says (each key read as the file types it, a whole number of any width the parser offers included, so no value type's limits decide; an integer the parser itself cannot read, past 128 bits, is its reason before any key, as any text it cannot parse), and the text is parsed for its syntax alone, the body included but skipped unread, so a body value the stamp never looks at is the reader's own to judge; and `stamp(text, noun)` for a reader that knows several versions and decides what to do with each (a known older one migrated, an unknown one refused, never guessed at; both keys required and of their type, the format first, a version of 0 read as it stands for the reader to decide). The four readers that wrote the header out by hand (the channel file C-017, the catalog C-028, the controller file C-030, the bench file C-033) the project file (C-046), the supervisão file and the binding file (C-048) and the release manifest (C-290) call it and map its refusals onto their own errors, so every reference's pinned refusal reads as before: their tests are the proof that nothing moved.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/src/schema.rs (unit) | a good header checked and stamped; not TOML with the noun in the text; no `format`, another format, no `version`, another version, each with its exact text; both keys missing and the keys inside a table refused for the format first; another format with a string version, and with versions of 2^64-1, 2^64 and 2^128-1, refused for its format; an integer `format` (one past 64 bits too), and a string, a fractional, a negative, an overflowing, a 64-bit, a 65-bit, a 128-bit and a negative 64-bit `version`, a boolean, an array, an inline table, a date and a nan, each named by its key in the not-TOML form; the largest version a `u32` holds checked; a version of 2^128 refused by the parser with its reason, no key named; a good header before a body that is not TOML refused with the parser's reason and no key named, and before a body value past `i64` checked; the stamp of another format read with both keys required, a version 0 read as it stands, the stamp without `format`, of an empty text, of an integer `format` and of a negative `version` refused the same way | and the readers' own tests unchanged: crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_schema.rs, crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_catalog.rs, crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/controller_rules.rs, crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/bench_file.rs
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-045.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-046: The project file: one `veredas-project` TOML the gateway runs, checked whole before anything starts, Veredas 1's folder read into it, every file of the suite held to its stamp
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files", "veredas-portal"; P-010 promoted; docs/project-schema.md)
- **Description:** A project is one TOML file, `format = "veredas-project"`, `version = 1`, read by core's reader (C-045) before its body, a misspelled key anywhere refused with its name (inside an inline table too: `start`, `store` and `active` are read key by key, a key missing from one and a shape that is neither word nor table named as well). It names the session; its `[session]` says where the logs start (`midnight`, a date-time, a date, `{ last_hours = N }`, or live when absent), where the store lives (`ram`; `{ disk = "<file>", memory_limit_mb }`, a file pinned by name, refused when one already stands there, C-041; or `{ dir = "<folder>", memory_limit_mb }`, a file of each run's own in that folder, named from the project's name and the session's start to the second, so every run leaves its own, `docs/questions.md` Q16; the two forms in one table refused naming both), whether writes are armed (off when absent) and the idle window; its `[server]` where to serve, or nothing; its `[secrets]` where the sealed values sit (`file`, beside the project, `secrets.toml` when absent, committed with it) and which environment variable holds the key that opens them (`key_env`), required once a source names a `secret`, refused by name otherwise and when its file or variable is blank, `docs/questions.md` Q15; its `[[log]]` sources are a `file` or a family (`dir` and `pattern`, with `date_format`, `active` and `recursive`), never both nor neither, each with an optional timestamp rule; its `[[rule]]` capture rules, `[[opc]]` sources (C-040's declarations) and `[[channel]]` channels (their channel file and catalog read from beside the project, their role `listen` with `bind` or `connect` with `host`, their reply rules checked against the catalog) and `[[table]]` views complete it. `project::load` resolves every relative path against the file's folder, compiles every rule once (a timestamp rule, a family spec, a capture rule, an OPC spec without its secret: the password is an entry of the secrets file, which the session opens when it starts), loads every referenced file, claims every table name once across the rules, the OPC rules, the catalogs' messages and the views (naming both owners when taken twice), requires every source id once and at least one source, and refuses everything else by name, so a project that loads is a session that can start (`Project::session_config`), bar what only the running pieces can tell (a view's SQL, which the store resolves when it opens, C-041; the secrets file and the key the environment holds when the session starts: the session opens the file in memory before it compiles a table and refuses by name, never by value, the variable unset or not a key, a file that is not a secrets file (by its path), a file the key does not open, and a source whose entry is not there, a file not there yet holding no entry so that an imported project's first run is refused by the entry its source names); the session (C-043) claims the same names, compiles the same tables and runs the channel's check again, through the same functions. A start in the past with a family that has no timestamp rule, a group name outside the name shape and a reply rule the channel's check refuses are refused too, by name. The executable `veredas-portal <project.toml>` runs it, serving when the project says so (on the address it bound, printed, the port the system's when asked for 0), printing every source's health once a second until stdin closes, then ending well; `--check` reads, checks and prints the plan without starting anything (neither the key nor the secrets file needed); `--new-key` prints a fresh key, 64 hexadecimal characters, and nothing else; `--secret <project.toml> <entry>` seals the value on stdin (to its end, the final line break dropped, LF or CRLF and nothing else: a bare carriage return and a line break inside kept) as that entry of the project's secrets file (its name trimmed), ChaCha20-Poly1305 under the key the project's variable holds with a fresh nonce each time and the entry's name bound in, the file created stamped `veredas-secrets` version 1 when none is there, printing the entry's name and the file's path and never the value, and refusing by name a project without `[secrets]`, a variable unset or not a key, a key that does not open the entries already there (nothing written then), and a file that is not a secrets file (every refusal of the file names its path, the stamp's with the suite's header text after it), a stray argument or an option given twice or in a name's place a usage error; `--from-v1 <folder>` reads a Veredas 1 project folder into this file on stdout and names on stderr what it did not carry (a disabled part, a telegram source without a catalog, an OPC source with no rule to map its tags, a rule whose scope names a log not carried, a view the engine refuses over the tables the file creates (the import loads the file as the gateway does, compiles its tables through the session's own compilation with a blank for every secret, and opens an in-memory store over them: a table nothing creates, a column no table has, whatever the shape of the SQL, is the engine's to say, and the view goes with the engine's reason) and a view over one of those, while a view over a file the engine cannot read where the import runs (a CSV on the plant's machine) is carried as written and named, and so is a view that reads it, directly or through other views, whatever their order in the file (the engine names every view it refuses at once, and the import judges them together, every set-aside view standing in as an empty table of its name so that whatever else a reader names is the engine's to bind: a reader that names a table nothing creates beside a set-aside view falls for that table whichever it names first, and a reader of a column only the file can show is carried and named as over a file not here, while a fault the import can judge in full, a column a table the file creates does not have, drops the reader with the reason and what reads it falls after; a reader refused for naming a view set aside, deferred, or set aside by its own reading in the same round is judged again against the stand-ins in the next (a cycle of readers falls, each with its reason), and one whose error names a set-aside view that already stands in, through a schema the stand-in is not in, falls with the reason, so every round decides a view and the judging ends), a security mode never implemented whatever shape its setting took, a certificate authentication never implemented, a store mode Veredas 1 never had, a memory limit on an in-memory store, the auto-export, the rules' skew-monitor threshold, the screens, the grep, the command sequencer), a credential-store password becoming a `secret` entry named for its source as a plain word (letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`, every other character `_`, every leading `-` `_` too so the tool never reads it as an option, a word already taken given `_2`, `_3`, so the call needs no shell's quoting; a source not carried names no entry and takes no word), the note giving the call that seals it, and the `[secrets]` table written with the suite's variable `VEREDAS_KEY`, an absolute start in either of Veredas 1's spellings (a `T` or a space between) carried in this file's own, one that is no date-time, or one with an offset (a date-time, but not on the plant's clock), named with the session starting live, a start of zero hours carried as one and named, a CSV-linked table a view over the engine's CSV reader, and a float-typed tag with bits an int word (bits are carved out of an int word, C-040), each named; it never guesses, and the file it prints loads, checked before it is printed: a folder that leaves nothing loadable is refused with the reason and the notes, never handed over as a file the gateway would then refuse. Law L7 (`tools/leis`): every TOML file of the repository that is not a tool's own (a cargo manifest, the test runner's `.config/nextest.toml`; `.toml` in any case), and every TOML block of a reference in `docs/` (a fence of three or more backticks or tildes whose info string's first word is `toml` in any case, whatever follows it, inside blockquotes and list items nested in any order as well (a list mark followed by a space or a tab), closed by a fence of its kind at least as long with nothing else on the line at the same quote depth, by a line with fewer quote marks than it was opened under (the blockquote ends there, as CommonMark has it), or by the end of the text; a reference's `.md` in any case), states a `veredas-*` `format` and a `version` from 1. The declarations the session reads (a log source, a timestamp rule, a capture rule) live in the project module, so the light build (no store, no server) still reads a project.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/project.rs | the worked example of docs/project-schema.md loaded beside the channel and controller references' files: its two logs (a dated family with its format, its newest active and its timestamp rule;
