## C-049: The host: the sessions of a supervisão file started through portal and served side by side, each under its name with its portal surface nested
- **Date:** 2026-11-09
- **Source:** user (docs/questions.md Q19, Q20; docs/supervisao-plan.md "Scope": the host; P-008 "several sessions served side by side from one machine")
- **Description:** The host starts every session of a loaded supervisão file through portal's library, serves the page, each session's binding, layout and object feed under `/s/<name>/` and the session's own portal router unchanged under `/s/<name>/portal/`, keeps every session's data its own, and stops them all when it stops; the executable runs a file, or checks it and prints the plan (`--check`). It runs the file C-048 reads (C-048 is the reading and its refusals, this clause the serving). It serves the page under a content policy that lets the browser load and run the page's own files alone, and the drawing's own route sandboxed, running and loading nothing but its embedded pictures, neither shown inside another page's frame (`frame-ancestors 'none'` in both, the architect's ruling of 2026-11-15; a page meant to frame supervisão's would have to be named there), every answer the router gives `nosniff`, whatever its status (the middleware checked over every status 100 to 999; every status the handlers write, read off the host's and portal's sources, reached by a request (a body over portal's limit 413, a read and a write with the plant gone 502 and 503) or named as the check's alone (500, 504)) (the HTTP layer's own refusal of a request it cannot read, a line it cannot parse or a header too large, a 400 or a 431 with no body, comes before any router and carries none, a limit) (Q23, decided): nothing a drawing points to on another host is loaded or run, whatever its syntax, the reader's refusals (C-048) the first layer and the policy the guarantee.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-supervisao/tests/host.rs | two sessions from two benches on one address: the page's content policy word for word on `/`, `/s/<name>/` and `/s/<name>`, the drawing's own policy (sandboxed) on its route, `nosniff` on every answer (every route the routers declare at every place the test asks it, its stream's head among them, a 404, a 405 for every route the routers declare asked with each method it does not declare (the root's, a session's and portal's nested), and every nested portal answer), and in a headless Chromium (Edge and Chrome both run here), every screen of the page as built refusing nothing under it, watched from its first load and read by the page's own report and by the browser's log, which keeps a refusal of a document since left (the sessions' list at `/`, and of every session the host lists every view its own tabs list, each drawn, a table and a series picked, the layout with an embedded picture in its drawing and the words of a session with none, an object selected with its sparkline, and every object of every session selected in turn, by its element or its attention row, its side panel built, the writes it offers among them, each pressed with the switch armed and its answer said; every control each view builds pressed in turn (every button: a layer, a zoom, a dashboard's tab, a table, its pages, a series, a write; every attention row); every element each view listens on for the pointer or the wheel (the page's own registrations, counted from its first load) driven through the browser's own input by each kind it listens for (moved over and off, pressed, dragged, the wheel turned both ways: a trend's hover, the drawing's pan and zoom, a keyed marker's clone among them), each heard there, and every kind of event the page's source listens for held to the way the test drives it (a registration the reading of the source cannot take failing it), and every kind the page registers as it runs, on the window, the document, an element or the feed, one the test drives; every ask the page's source makes (read off it, an ask the reading cannot take failing it: an address not written as a string there, a WebSocket, a beacon, an `XMLHttpRequest`, a module loaded at run time) refused by the browser in turn, the screen it fails to drawn (the sessions' list and a session's page failing to start, the feed, a dashboard, a table's rows, a series' points and a sparkline, a write's post, the lists of tables and of series), each view shown and driven as above, each ask refused by the browser's own report (a limit: each view, each input and each ask's failure driven once, at one window size (1440 by 900), not their combinations, nor every state a timer reaches mid-change, the dashboards' ten-second give-up, a refresh racing a change of view, nor what an observer (of a resize, of the document's changes) runs at another size); a session's card from the list and the session picker to the other session, each leaving the page; the plant moving under a watched page (an object's state changed, the strip's alarm line drawn) and the feed's loss and return as the page's own feed reports them (its `error` and `open` dispatched on it: a browser offline keeps a stream it holds); and a file of one session, `/` handing over to its page); then each of twenty-nine kinds of load and script (an SVG image, an HTML image, a style's background and cursor, a paint, a filter, a mask, a clip path and a marker from another document in a `style` attribute, a paint in a presentation attribute, a picture in a filter, a `use` of another document, a script element, a script and a handler written inline, a sheet linked and one written inline importing another, a frame, an object, a font, a video, an audio, a manifest, a prefetch hint, a fetch, a feed, a WebSocket, a beacon, a module imported), each alone: in a blank document with no policy it asks a listener standing for another host or runs (the probe live), and put straight into the mounted page it asks nothing of it and runs nothing, refused by its own address (or `inline`, or the host's origin where the browser strips a frame's path) under its directive; a kind the browser may ask nothing for even with no policy named (a cursor, and a paint, a filter, a mask, a clip path, a marker and a `use` from another document, a manifest; Edge here asks for all but the filter, the `use` and the manifest), held then to asking nothing under the policy rather than to its own refusal; the drawing opened alone at its route of no origin, each of its fifteen kinds live in the same drawing served with no policy from this machine (but those of its six references to another document a browser asks nothing for even with no policy, named and held to asking nothing alike: a filter and a `use` in Edge here, a limit where another browser, CI's, differs) and asking and running nothing in it, the drawing waited for through the protocol's page events and opened ten times more; Inkscape's script beside a mesh in the drawing (Q27: the reader drops it), its code asking the other host, live when the drawing is served with no policy and asking nothing from any of the page's screens or from the drawing alone; the page and the drawing framed by a page of another origin, each refused by the browser's report of `frame-ancestors 'none'` (one per frame, naming the host's origin, as the browser names a frame) and neither document in its frames, a file of the host's with no policy framed beside them shown; the page and its bundle with their types at `/`, `/s/<name>/` and `/s/<name>` (both sessions, both spellings), the sessions in the file's order, every other path 404 by its path (every route the two routers declare, read from their own source as literal `.route` declarations, every other way a router takes a route (`route_service`, `nest_service`, `merge`, `fallback_service`) absent from both and every path not a literal named, so none is left out: each of portal's answering under each session's `/portal` and a 404 at the root, under a `/portal` of no session and under a session without its `/portal`; the root's own (those its source declares before its loop over the sessions, its page aside) a 404 under a session, under `/portal`, under a session's `/portal` and under no session; each of a session's own answering under the session and a 404 at the root, under a `/portal` and under a session that is not there); each session's binding view (the objects, rules, writes and panels as the page reads them: every object of both sessions, every field the page reads, as its binding holds it, a marker's path and an object's key among them; every rule's comparison as the test writes it, its numbers spelled by the test (never by the code's own `format_number`; the view writes a rule's number in full, as the binding writes it, one of them 0.0625, which the screen's three places would write otherwise), each comparison a rule takes (`Compare`, matched in full) met in a view, every one of them and every form of another series (a key of its own, the object's key inherited, no key) on an object with a unit, each series a rule reads (its own left to it, its own written out, another, another with the key it takes) met in a view, named bare when it is another; every way of showing and every state a rule names spelled as the binding file writes them, read from the file, never from the code's names, and each of the reader's own lists of them met in a view), its layout or a 404 naming the session without one, its portal surface nested (the session's own name and switch, a tag read, portal's own event stream opening with its hello) and unchanged: portal's own router over the session's live state, served on a listener of the test, answering every route portal declares (read from its source) as the nested one does, status and body, a stream's head and first event, the clocks' now aside, its posts among them, and its head header for header (the date and a length aside), `nosniff` the one header the host adds; a value set on one plant reaching its session and never the other, each session's object stream opening with its own snapshot and then carrying its own plant's change and never the other's (each stream read on past the other plant's change), the object stream driven through a lag of its own (two events sent where it holds one): its snapshot on connect, a snapshot again once it fell behind, every event by its own name, together the names its source holds; every event the feed sends (its names read from its source, each sent under a literal name: a snapshot, an objects event, a status) told apart between the sessions by a value that differs (a plant's value, the switch, armed on one session and not the other, in its statuses, its snapshots, its `objects.json` and portal's own session); the dashboards per session, each from its own plant and its own store: the plants set apart on every series a panel reads, every panel of the dashboard (one of every kind the reader knows, from its own refusal) differing between the sessions over the same window, the levels' outlet 1 at 0.4 and 0.7; each session's dashboard equal, whole, to the one computed in process over its own store, its own binding and its own feed's snapshot (`Host::snapshot`), so a panel read from the other session's store is told apart; and a missing one by name; a write carrying its content landing through hall's nested router in hall's plant alone, loop's switch refusing the same post and loop's plant keeping its value (portal's routes are compared on one request each, no query and an empty object posted; writes with content in full are C-054's); the stop with an object stream and portal's own stream open returning at once, ending every source of both cleanly, ending both streams and freeing the address; a session that does not start named with its reason, an address taken refused before any session starts on that same file (the address answered, never the session) and before the first starts (its disk store never created), and `--check` starting nothing, in each way the executable takes it (after the file and before it, `main.rs`'s two arms counted) (exit 0, both sessions planned in the file's order, nothing served, no store created), and so with the file's own address held (its host running, say: a check binds nothing); the executable printing its address and both sessions in the file's order, serving them, a line on stdin no close (one not in UTF-8, an empty one with or without its carriage return; a read that fails, as a terminal hanging up, also stops it, a limit no test here drives), and stopping when stdin closes even with a stream open (exit 0 at once, the stream ended, its sessions stopped: a disk store closed, no write-ahead log left); a stop waiting for what is still computed (a dashboard of two panels over a view that reads for seconds, asked a second before the stop, the host's runtime dropped only once its blocking work ends: nothing left holding the session's live state when the stop returns, the disk store closed, no write-ahead log left; portal's own session stop waits behind the store's reads as well, so a host cutting its wait at two seconds stays unseen by this test, the wait now the host's own); refusing a held address and a session that does not start with exit 2 and the reason on stderr. Limits: the policy is the browser's to enforce (the claim is for a browser that implements it), and the claim rests on the policy's text and the browser's own reading of each load by a directive, driven for the kinds the test lists (a worker, an `xml-stylesheet` instruction written into the drawing opened alone and the like are not); the browser may open a bare connection to another host, asking nothing of it, which the policy does not govern (its preconnect for a frame before the policy refuses the frame, a `preconnect` hint; a `dns-prefetch` asks a resolver, which no listener here sees); a navigation is no load the policy bars (a link's on a click, and one with no click, a `meta` refresh, which the policy lets go to another host): a drawing carries none past the reader (the SVG namespace alone, C-048) and the page's own reading (an XML parse, its `clean`), not past the policy; portal's nested surface answers with its own headers (`nosniff` added); the host and portal read no request's `Host` or `Origin`, so a name re-pointed at the host's address (DNS rebinding) is the page's own origin to a browser, which no content policy governs (Q29: left by the architect to the suite's security round, P-014, which names a second way, a write a page of any site posts as `text/plain`); two sessions stand for several (the host serves them in one loop with no path of their own by number), one driven apart (`/` handing over to it); a stop waits for every computation still running, with no bound, the dashboards' asks a page has given up on among them (a dashboard slower than the page's ten seconds piles up asks while the page is open; C-052's side); a new stream client misses no change because the route subscribes before it reads the snapshot and the feed writes its snapshot before it sends the change, an order no socket test can force, read from the code; a client that falls behind gets its own session's snapshot because the stream reuses the route's own (no host test lags a live stream). Not promised: Ctrl+C or SIGTERM end the executable without stopping its sessions (a disk store's write-ahead log is left behind); the stop is stdin's end

## C-050: The object feed: every object's value, state, staleness and place, evaluated live in Rust
- **Date:** 2026-11-09

- **Description:** Per session, every object's latest value from portal's frames and caches (a text or a time from the cache alone, as the ingest renders it, an empty text a value; two samples under one stamp ordered by the ingest's count of the series whichever way each came, so the same sample read twice is no new arrival and a later line whose frame a lag lost is taken from the cache for the series' value, a key's own value and place coming from frames alone), its state (a class with a mark and a word, by the first rule that holds, a rule over another series reading that series as its type), stale when it has no value, its source is away (idle, retrying, ended or failed, or waiting again once it has delivered) or its value is older than its bound, and a marker's place along its path (one per key of a keyed series, each with its own value as shown, a key forgotten past its bound), served as a snapshot and a stream of changes (a change is anything but the arrival time) with the session's switch and health, a status at least every second on its ticks' grid (a late tick never moving the grid: the code's choice, `MissedTickBehavior::Skip`, which no test here measures apart from a loaded machine's own lateness); the feed reads on past a lag of the session's frames.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-supervisao/tests/feed.rs | on the fold with a clock the test holds: no value read as stale, a level's fill and state for six values at and across its rules' edges (clamped), an older sample never replacing a newer, a later one under an equal stamp doing so and the same sample again or an earlier line under it not, a source away keeping the value as stale, codes by equals, a rule over another series taking precedence, decimals, a freshness bound fresh at it and stale past it, markers from data frames (an unread series, a malformed frame and a sample without its value or its count changing nothing), clamped, none without a value, markers per key, each with its own value as shown (at its decimals, its place clamped), forgotten past the bound and past 10 s with none, the value of a keyed series its latest sample whatever its key, a series keyed by the cache's count (one key or two) before any frame showing no single place, a key's older sample no new arrival, an object bound to one key (a marker's one place, a value's own state, other keys never reaching it), text through the cache compared exactly, a newer cached number healing a missed frame and an older one ignored, a value, a level, a place or a marker changing being a change and the same reading arriving again not, NaN the same as itself, a state alone changing being a change (the source away, past the bound, flipped by another series with the value unchanged), the first rule taken where a lighter one comes before a weightier, `below` strict at its bound, a level and a place in ranges not starting at 0 (50..150, -50..50) at and past their ends, a rule over a text series no object reads comparing "0" as text and never as the number 0, a time from the cache kept as rendered when a frame brings its epoch milliseconds, a rule bound to a key reading that key's sample after another key moved, a key's later sample at an equal index replacing the one before with its new arrival and the same sample again keeping it, nothing stored read as no value and an empty text taken as a value with its state, a new sample under the same stamp taken while the same entry polled again keeps its arrival, under one stamp by the ingest's count: two frames with the cache trailing at the first (the second kept with its frame's arrival, stale past its bound from it), a cache behind a frame after a lag lost two, the cache read before its own frame and a later line then taken from it, two keys framed with the cache trailing at the first, the six health labels, the page's form of a view, the six marks; live, against the bench and a written log over the snapshot route and the stream: every object of the plant as its rules read it, the session's switch and health, the ## C-052: The dashboards: every panel kind computed in Rust from the session's store and drawn
- **Date:** 2026-11-09
- **Source:** user (docs/design/supervisao/ "Dashboard objects"; docs/binding-schema.md "A panel")
- **Description:** Each panel of a binding's dashboards is computed from the session's store into what the page draws (a value tile in four states, its sub-line the rule that named its state as the feed picked it (one over another series naming that series and its key, its comparison bare), a number a person wrote (a rule's, a target, a trend's limit, a bars' target, a cells' spread) said in full, as the binding writes it, by the host and drawn as the host wrote it, a trend with its limit and breaches, bars per clock-aligned bucket (a limit: a width that divides a day from midnight, another from the epoch, a week from a Thursday) against a target with the running one marked, each from 0 up to its value or down to it, state over time (a run one state, its class and its word together; the state holding at the window's start drawn from it; a rule written out over the object's own series its own; an object whose rule reads another series refused on a timeline, Q24), every chart of an object bound to one key reading that key's samples alone, a state grid with the count of its cells not normal, levels against ticks, a table with inline bars (a value's magnitude against the column's largest, a column with rules keeping its state and word beside its bar) and state words, a time written as the store's clock reads it (a timestamp of any unit or zone to the second, a date as its day, a time of day with its zone or without to the second, the day's end (24:00:00) as itself (a limit: portal turns a stamp finer than a millisecond into milliseconds toward 1970, so a time before 1970 within a millisecond of its second is written at the next second; a limit: the engine's driver hands over a time of day with its zone as its local time alone, so its offset is not read; a time past the clock's reach, beyond the year 262 143 either way, the engine's infinities among them, is written as its milliseconds): every date and time type the engine lists but the interval, which portal writes as text; a limit: a time of day in nanoseconds, which the engine's driver cannot read, leaves its panel saying so), an integer cell in its own digits however large, a column read by its engine type (in a numeric one the digits of an integer past 64 bits a number, its places written as zeros (none, and no point, at 0 places, up to the reader's 6), its bar and its rules reading it, a rule through the double nearest it, a limit: a threshold is a double too; in any other a string text, sorted as text; a boolean 1 or 0 to its rules, its bar and its sort, written true or false), charts in cells (a value column that is no number refused in the panel's place) with a median, its sparkline, a distribution on one scale, σ and n, each row of the window counted once while rows arrive, read in order by its stamp, source and sequence and told from another by every column (so a view fanning a stored row into several keeps each; a view as its table must carry `index_ms`, `source` and `seq`; a limit: a view's rows alike in every column are one), a number key by its full value (an integer however large, its keys in their exact order) and a time key as the clock reads it, the keys in the key column's own order by its engine type (each NUMERIC type the engine lists, a time by the clock, a boolean false before true, any other type as text, an ENUM's labels among them, a text column's digits as text), a row with no key or no value charted in no row and counted beside the window, a table's sort exact for whole numbers (never through a double), the first rule that holds naming a state on a timeline and in a table as on a tile (limits: a DECIMAL is read as the double nearest it, portal's conversion, and a UHUGEINT as the engine's driver hands it over, a double), capped said only when the cap left a row of the window unread; a panel that cannot be computed (refused or panicking) saying why in its place, the others drawn; a limit: the windows, buckets and runs sit on the store's axis, the plant's wall clock read as UTC (C-041), so across a change of the plant's clock an hour repeats or is skipped, and midnight is the wall clock's) and the page draws it, computing nothing but its drawing: positions, and its axes' labels from the window and values the host gave it (a trend's four values and five times, its axis from the lowest to the highest of its points and its limit, 0 to 1 with neither; bars' top and bottom values and 0 between them, the axis from the lowest to the highest of 0, the values and the target, 0 to 1 when all are 0; buckets' starts and target, a timeline's start), its hover texts (a bar's, a run's, a grid cell's) as the host gave them, a value missing drawn as missing on every surface the host sends one to (a tile's figure, a grid cell's, a level's fill and figure), a sample that is no finite number (NaN, an infinity) no value, left out of every chart and every bucket, each chart's left edge room enough for its longest value label, a rule's word on a tile's badge as written, every text as written (none the stylesheet generates or changes: the page's own headings typed in capitals, a label, a column name and σ as written, Q28), a window of one point drawn as a dot on a sparkline as on a trend, and an answer for a dashboard no longer shown dropped (another dashboard's panels never standing under a tab: until its answer comes, the grid says the dashboard is asked), a refusal of it alike, a refusal on time said in the dashboard's place, the five-second refresh (each ask five seconds after the last, measured) never asking while an answer is still out, and giving an ask up after ten seconds with no answer, said in the dashboard's place, the next tick asking again. It reads the store through C-041's queries (numeric windows, pages of rows) and adds the panels' computation and drawing; the store itself is C-041's.

## C-054: Writes, gated: offered by the binding, shown gated, refused by portal's switch on the wire
- **Date:** 2026-11-09
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "every write rides portal's gated surface"; docs/design/README.md "writes shown as gated")
- **Description:** An object's actions are shown gated and disabled while the session's switch is off, and before the page holds any status; a press asks for and posts the binding's own action, the one pressed (its value, to its source); a post to the session's portal write route through supervisão's address is refused 403 by the switch with nothing reaching the plant, and lands at the plant once the project arms the switch; supervisão holds no write path of its own and never decides the switch's position, at start or after. A press whose post never leaves is said, and its button is the switch's again; a press's answer is said under the object pressed, whatever is selected when it comes (another object, with actions or none, or nothing), and again whenever that object is selected, the last answer of each of its actions in a line of its own, in the actions' order (two of one label being two actions, each by its place among them), so one action's answer never overtakes another's, and an action whose post is out stays busy however often its object is selected again, never pressed twice at once, as portal means it: written (the plant took it), refused (by the switch, a value or a body portal cannot read, or the plant's own status), not sent (503: the plant never saw it), not confirmed (504: it may have reached the plant) or no answer (portal's answer never came whole, came unreadable, or came in no form of portal's, a code it never refuses a write with or a success that says no `ok`: it may have reached the plant). Limits: a page cannot tell a post that never left from one whose answer was lost on the way back (a request fails alike), so a press with no answer is said as having none, the write perhaps landed, never as not sent; a post is given no deadline of the page's own, so one whose answer neither comes nor fails is said nothing, its button busy, until the browser fails it; the answers live in the page's memory, so a reload, another session's page or another browser says none of them, and so does its guard: one of those can press an action whose post is out, and portal takes both posts; an answer is known as portal's by its form alone, so something between the page and portal answering in portal's form (a proxy's 503 `{ error }` after it forwarded the post) is said as portal's; a press answered not confirmed (504) frees its button though the write may still land when the link comes back, so a second press may write twice; armed, portal's route answers any client of the address (it checks no origin and no content type, portal's surface), and the page's question before a write is the page's own step, no gate on the wire; Limits: the buttons follow the switch at the feed's status (about a second behind, and the last status kept while the feed is down), so portal's refusal on the wire is the guarantee; core's members are judged by path, not by name (none of them dials or writes but through a path judged); a path written inside an attribute's string (serde's `default`, `with`) is no token the lexer reads; and of the HTTP methods, POST, PUT, DELETE and PATCH alone are swept.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-supervisao/tests/writes.rs, crates/veredas-supervisao/tests/component.rs (every file the crate compiles and no build script, read as C-047 reads them: recorded with this clause) | every answer portal gives a write said under the object pressed as it means, through a link the test freezes and closes: a value of `on` posted byte for byte as the binding writes it and landing as 1, a value the tag cannot take (400), a body past portal's limit (413), the plant's own refusal of a read-only tag (200, not good), not confirmed while the link is frozen (504, core's `WriteError` matched in full and portal's own code for each) and landing once it thaws, not sent once the link is closed (503) with nothing at the plant; every object pressed, selected again, saying the last answer of each of its actions pressed, a line each in its actions' order; two actions of one label on one object pressed together, both asked and posted with their own bodies, each answer in its own line, every line in the actions' order after answers came out of it, each drawn below the one before; the page's list of portal's refusal codes held to portal's own (a value or a body it cannot read, a body past its limit, and `refused_code` of core's refusals); after portal has answered and the write landed, a success saying no `ok` and a gateway's code in portal's own form each said as no answer; and through a stand-in for the page's own fetch of the write (nothing reaching the plant), every status a response may carry (200 to 599) with every form of body (portal's refusal `{ error }`, portal's answer `{ ok }` true and false, another shape of JSON, a body that is no JSON, none) said as no answer but in portal's own pairs (its refusal under its own codes, its answer under a 200), where each is said as it means, each of portal's codes with a body of every other shape (none, another key, an error no string, a string, a list, null) said as no answer, a 200 saying `ok` as no boolean said as no answer, and portal's two forms of a 200 said written and refused; outlet 3's release and hold both out at once, its panel built again while they are out showing both buttons busy with the switch on (the release forced live and clicked asking nothing), the busy guard keyed by the object and the action's place on every panel a button stands on (outlet 3's release out read on the panel it was pressed on after a status, busy and off; ## C-070: The controller's state text: one writer, one reader, the table of reasons
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026; docs/controlador-plan.md "The state"); promoted in part from P-018
- **Description:** `veredas_core::state` holds a controller's state as structured data (layer 1 with at most one reason of its own state, the start-up phase, the conditions, the maintenance requests) beside the table of reason, phase and maintenance numbers and words as the plan states them. Its one writer spells a state as one line (`docs/state-text.md`); its one reader accepts exactly the texts the writer writes and refuses every other spelling by name and byte, so a text read and written back is the same characters and a state written and read back is the same state (its maintenance requests in the table's order). Each value has one spelling, a TOML value TOML's own parser reads the same; health is derived as the plan says. The readings the plan left open are `docs/questions.md` Q100's.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/state_text.rs | the plan's table and seven examples, read and written back byte for byte; every reason, phase and condition in its order, an absent one unwritten; health; every Unicode character in a string, spelled as the list says and read the same by `toml`; every term of a seeded corpus of 4,000 states read by `toml` as the state holds it; round trips both ways; every one-character deletion, insertion and replacement in 600 of its texts either refused or written back unchanged; spellings the writer never writes refused by name, the messages pinned

## C-071: The Modbus/TCP client: every function of a device's data model, bounded, every answer checked
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: the four fieldbus masters in core, the Modbus TCP client on existing crates; docs/controlador-plan.md "Networks and devices"); promoted in part from P-018
- **Description:** `veredas_core::fieldbus::modbus::Client` (behind core's feature `modbus`, off by default) speaks every public function of a Modbus/TCP device's data model (the four reads, a coil or a register written alone or several at once, a register masked, registers read and written in one request) in the standard's frames. It refuses a request outside the standard's bounds before anything is sent, bounds connecting and every answer in time, and takes an answer only when it answers its request (transaction, unit, function, quantity, a write's echo); otherwise it closes the connection, so a late answer is never read as another request's, and the next call connects again. A device's exception keeps the connection. A write says what the device may have done: never sent, refused by the device, or unconfirmed. Met against the bench's Modbus server (C-110) from the test-only tool `tools/mestres`, as `docs/questions.md` Q101 takes it (`docs/core-plan.md`, "The fieldbus masters").
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/modbus_client.rs, tools/mestres/tests/modbus.rs | over real TCP, a peer recording the bytes: every function's request and answer the specification's own example frames, byte for byte; exceptions kept apart and the connection kept; a silent device ended at the bound for a read and unconfirmed for a write, the connection closed and the next call reconnecting; a late answer never read as the next; a closed and a cut link; thirteen ways an answer does not answer (transaction, unit, function, quantity of words and of bytes of bits, every echo, a coil neither on nor off), each closing the connection; every bound refused before a connection and the limits taken; a refused port and a full listen queue bounded; drop and disconnect closing; the unit carried; against the bench's Modbus server, a device written apart from the client: every function reading what the plant side set and writing what it then holds (the mask's arithmetic, a read-write's overlap, the largest requests at a table's end), the server's own record of each request the specification's bytes with the transaction counting from 0, an address past a table and a unit not played answered as the device's no with the connection kept and nothing written, a slow device landing a write the client calls unconfirmed and a silent one not landing it, a restarted device ending the call and the next connecting again, a stopped one never connected
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-071.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-150: orcl-std — ORCL's standard control library, carried from Veredas 1
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "From Veredas 1 crates to Veredas 2"; docs/orcl/README.md); promoted from P-003 (the ORCL crates)
- **Description:** `crates/orcl-std`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, is the library every ORCL program builds on, as Veredas 1 left it (`docs/orcl/README.md`, "What it absorbs from Veredas 1"): `no_std`, `unsafe` forbidden, the panic lints denied. The scan model: a `Scan` carries the scan's time, read once, its period and its cycle number; a `Program` scans copyable inputs into copyable outputs (a program whose inputs or outputs are not `Copy` does not compile, whatever other traits they have, and nothing beyond `Copy` and `Default` is asked) and declares safe outputs, all-default unless it says otherwise. Every timer takes its time from the scan's time, never from its cycle number, and each block's `q()` reads what its last scan returned. The on-delay `Ton` turns on once its input has been on for `pt`, exactly at `pt`, its elapsed time capped at `pt`, and turns off and restarts from zero with the input; the off-delay `Tof` holds on for `pt` after its input drops, its elapsed time then held at `pt`; the pulse `Tp` gives one pulse of exactly `pt` from a rising edge whatever the input does, ignores a retrigger during it, holds its elapsed time at `pt` while the input stays on and resets once it is off, on the scan the pulse ends if the input is off by then. The edges: `RTrig` on for exactly one scan per rising edge, every one of them, a signal already on at the first scan counting as one; `FTrig` on for one scan per falling edge, every one of them, never at start. The up counter `Ctu` counts rising edges, a held input once (a reset released with the input high counts nothing), reset winning over counting, saturating at its top instead of overflowing, `q` on while the count reaches the preset, judged on every scan against the preset that scan is given (a preset of zero or below at once, a preset raised above the count turning it off, and at its top). `Bits` reads and writes bit `n` of `u8` to `u64`, a bit written with the value it holds staying as it was, an index past the width reading false and writing nothing. Function blocks (timers, edges, the counter) are neither `Copy` nor `Clone`, so scanning a copy does not compile (ORCL's O14). The crate's root states `no_std`, `forbid(unsafe_code)` and the six denied lints, and nothing that undoes them; its manifest names no other root, and no file of the crate brings in another crate. The list of types O13 forbids is not carried while law L7 refuses a `clippy.toml` (`docs/questions.md` Q225).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-std/src/timers.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/edges.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/counters.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/bits.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/lib.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/scan.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/o14.rs, crates/orcl-std/tests/blocks.rs | `cargo test -p orcl-std`, then `cargo clippy -p orcl-std --all-targets -- -D warnings`, then `cargo build -p orcl-std --target x86_64-unknown-none`, one command whose exit code is the verdict (the panic lints are clippy's, which no other run of the suite calls; the build for a target with no standard library proves `no_std` for every file, the target installed through rustup): Veredas 1's ten tests (each timer's exact switching times, an interrupted on-delay restarting from zero, a retriggered pulse, both edges over a five-scan trace, counting with a held input and a reset, the counter saturating at `i32::MAX`, bits set and cleared, an index past the width); then every timer still in its old state 1 ms before `pt` and switched at it, and switched on the same scan at a `pt` of zero, every timer started and read on scans whose time disagrees with their cycle number, an off-delay holding for `pt` after a second drop, scanned late with its elapsed time capped at `pt` and held there, a pulse triggered for one scan lasting exactly `pt`, its elapsed time held at `pt` while the input stays on, every block's `q()` against its scans, a rising edge from a signal on at the first scan, the reset beating a rising edge in the same scan and a released reset counting nothing, `q` off below the preset, on from it and past it, on at a preset of zero or below, and on at `i32::MAX`, every width's top bit read and written and nothing past it by `with_bit` or `set_bit`, a program's safe outputs all-default and one that declares its own, the crate root compared line by line with what it must hold, so nothing is commented out, allowed back or brought in; a bit written with the value it holds on every width; a reset released low counting the next edge; a pulse of zero length off at once and an edge on the scan a pulse ends starting nothing; six falling edges; every timer held until a preset time of 100.5 ms; the counter's `q()` below its preset; reads far past the width; the crate's version; a pulse ending with its input off reset on that scan and the next edge pulsing again; the counter's `q` following a preset raised and lowered; six rising edges; the manifest naming no other root and no source file bringing in a crate, every file of `src/` read; a program on an `f32` and on a structure that is only `Copy` and `Default`, which compiles; and `compile_fail` doctests: for each of the six blocks scanning a copy and a clone, and a program with inputs or outputs that are a `Vec`, everything but `Copy`, each beside a twin that compiles, since stable rustdoc does not check the error code a `compile_fail` names, so an unrelated error cannot keep one green
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-150.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-151: orcl-runtime — the engine of a scan: one cyclic task on the clock its host hands it
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-controlador: the softPLC"; docs/controlador-plan.md "Faults"; docs/orcl/README.md "The engine of a scan"); promoted from P-003 (the deterministic scan)
- **Description:** `crates/orcl-runtime`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, runs one `Program` as a cyclic task on a clock its host hands in, with no operating-system code: virtual time (`VirtualClock`: still until moved, a wait moving it to the deadline at once, never back, clones sharing one time) or a target's monotonic clock (`MonoClock`: a wait never returning before its deadline). A task refuses a period of zero by name. Scan `k` is due at `k` periods from the start and starts there, whatever the scans before it took; its inputs are read once at its start, the program is handed that snapshot, the scan's time and its outputs as the last scan left them, and its outputs are written once at its end. A scan ends when its outputs are handed to the plant: its length runs from its start to that moment, the program's own run included, and the time-error rule and the deadlines it skipped are judged at that same moment; the plant's own time to take the outputs is not the scan's. A deadline found passed when the task is stepped again, whether it passed during the plant's write or while the host was away, runs late, its lateness counted, and every other deadline passed by the end of that late scan is skipped and counted, never caught up. A scan longer than its period is a time error, counted and reported with the scan, its outputs still written; a scan of exactly the period is none; the rule measures a scan's length alone, never how late it started. A deadline that passed while a scan ran is skipped and counted, never caught up, and one that falls exactly at the scan's end runs then. A scan longer than twice its period faults the task (a watchdog fault naming the scan's length, at the scan's start): that scan's own outputs are never written, the safe outputs are, and from then on every scan reads its inputs and writes the safe outputs without scanning the program, so the plant's exchange goes on; the faulting scan is a time error, no later one is, nor an overrun, and the fault keeps the cause and the date of the scan that raised it. The rule is measured when a scan ends; a scan that never ends is the host's watchdog's to catch. The statistics count scans, overruns and skipped cycles, and a scan's lateness against its deadline and its length, with their maxima and means. The same program, inputs and times give the same scans. One cyclic task only: several tasks wait for the execution model (`docs/questions.md` Q33, Q223).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-runtime/tests/scan_cycle.rs, crates/orcl-runtime/tests/conveyor.rs, crates/orcl-runtime/tests/mono_clock.rs | on virtual time: a zero period refused with its text; 100 scans of 10 ms before 1 s, each on its exact deadline with 3 ms of work, reading what the one before wrote, every read and write logged at its exact time, the statistics exact; a scan of exactly one period; a 15 ms scan skipping one deadline and a 20 ms scan (twice the period, the limit itself) skipping one and ending on the next, which runs at once; the longest scan's length and the mean pinned where lengths differ; a scan of 20 ms and 1 ns faulting, every write that reached the plant listed (one per scan, at its end, the program's own safe outputs, which no default gives, from the faulting scan on), the deadlines it overran skipped and the program scanned no more; a host back late, the scan handed its start and not its deadline, its lateness counted with its maximum, total and mean; a fault dated at a late start; a program that itself runs 15 ms (a time error, its outputs written at its end) or 25 ms (the fault, the faulting scan a time error); a scan started 8 ms late running 5 ms (no time error) and one started 9 ms late running 15 ms (a time error, no fault, its own outputs written); a faulted scan started late reading at its start with its lateness counted; a faulted scan of 25 ms neither a time error nor an overrun, the fault keeping its first date and length; a program that writes its output on even cycles alone, the odd ones writing what it left, and one that writes on a first scan that is a time error, the next writing what it left; a host back 25 ms late (no time error, its lateness counted whole); a scan of 10 ms and 1 ns (a time error); a later faulted scan of 30 ms leaving the fault's 25 ms; the faulting and faulted scans' lengths in the maximum, total and mean; a virtual wait of 10 s taking no time on the wall; cycles past 2^32 each on its deadline; a period of zero refused naming its task (`fast`); a plant whose write takes 1 ms after a scan of exactly 10 ms (no time error, the next deadline run 1 ms late, nothing skipped) and after one of 9.5 ms (the next deadline run at 10.5 ms); with a slow write, the statistics' length and overruns, a 19.5 ms scan no fault and a 25 ms one a fault naming 25 ms; lateness late twice (3 ms maximum, 5 total, 1.25 mean); a host back 25 ms late and a write spanning two deadlines, each running the first late and skipping the others passed by that scan's end, every cycle run or counted; two runs alike; Veredas 1's conveyor program and its five tests, then its 22 s against its demo plant on the engine, every change of state, of the alarm and of the count on its exact scan; on a target's clock, waits never early and scans never before their deadlines, every cycle run or counted as skipped
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-151.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-110: The Modbus server: a device's four tables on a real TCP socket
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: bench devices for the four fieldbuses, a Modbus server among them; P-020; docs/maquinar-plan.md "P-020: the bench devices, as they land")
- **Description:** `modbus::start` serves a `ModbusConfig` (TOML, every optional key defaulted, a misspelled key refused; a bench file's `[[modbus]]`, C-033) as a Modbus/TCP server on its own thread and runtime, the bench's own code: four tables sized by the file (at most 65536 addresses; absent, reaching the last point) and named points in them (a bool in a bit table; a `u16`, `i16`, `u32`, `i32` or `f32` in a register table, the two-register ones in either word order), refused by name before anything binds when a point has no name or another's, a type or a word order it cannot take, an address past its table or over another point, or a value that does not parse; it answers function codes 01 to 06, 0F, 10, 16 and 17 as the protocol specification's worked examples answer, byte for byte, each request the moment it is read and in its order (pipelined, split across segments, several connections at once), every other function with exception 01, a length, quantity, byte count or coil value the function does not take with 03 before an address past its table with 02 (a refused request writes nothing), a unit other than the one it plays with 0B, 0 and 255 included (none configured, every unit is the device, as a client addressing a device on TCP directly with 255 expects), a frame of another protocol identifier dropped unanswered and a length no frame has closing the connection; the plant side reads and sets every table by point (typed or as text) and by address, inputs included, each side reading next what the other wrote; its knobs hold every answer for a delay (a connection's pipelined answers one after another), withhold every answer not yet sent (a request read while silent, or an answer still waiting out its delay, never sent later), and close every open connection (those it serves, those still in its listen queue, one blocked on a client that reads nothing; returning once they are, the server listening on), a connection limit closing one more at once; every request, every withheld answer and every refused connection counted in a status that keeps the last hundred exchanges; stop and drop close every connection and free the port, a taken port refused naming the address. A client library the bench did not write reads and writes every table through it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/modbus_server.rs | the configuration's defaults and every key read, a misspelled key and an unknown table or type word refused; values per type with their limits; every refusal of a configuration with its exact message, the largest tables and a point at the last address accepted, and a bad value refused before a taken port is reached; the specification's examples for 01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 0F, 10, 16 and the 02 exception byte for byte with the header checked (transaction and unit echoed, protocol 0, the length), what each write left read on the plant side, 17's write before its read (overlapping too); every exception in the protocol's order at each function's limits (2000 bits, 125 registers, 1968 coils, 123 and 121 registers, a coil value, wrong lengths and byte counts, an empty table, the top address not wrapping) with nothing a refused write names landing; 0B for every other unit and every unit served without one; the points' registers as the wire carries them in both word orders, the sizes taken, a NaN read back; `tokio-modbus` reading every table and type, writing by every write function with the plant side seeing it, and the reverse, its exceptions; three requests in one segment and one split byte by byte answered in order and once, five connections at once; another protocol dropped and a length of 0, 1, 255 or 65535 closing, the 254 a frame can hold read whole; a delay held on every answer (two in turn, two pipelined the second after the first), silence never answered and the next answer the next request's, silence set while an answer waits out its delay withholding it, every connection dropped (one waiting out a 30 s delay too) and a new one served, fifty connections dropped straight after their connect returned (closed, never answered) and one whose client reads nothing dropped within a second; the last hundred of 150 exchanges kept; refused writes of coils and registers over values none of them carries, nothing changed; a limit of two closing a third at once; stop freeing the port; the reference's drive bench run through the bench file.

## C-152: veredas-controlador — the controller's states and the commands that move them, over one cyclic task
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/controlador-plan.md "Start, stop and power", "The commissioning switch", "Faults", "The state", "Rules"); promoted in part from P-003
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-controlador`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, a library, holds a controller running one ORCL program as one cyclic task on `orcl-runtime`'s engine (C-151), its state core's structured `ControllerState` (C-070) and its text core's. It comes up from nothing in STOP; after a power cycle, RUN (and a start-up a power cut interrupted) returns through a warm start with no person, and STOP, HOLD and FAULT return as `STOP power_on(was)`; a runtime that restarted while the machine did not comes up in `FAULT internal`. A start, warm or cold, is a person's (`oficina` or `local`) with the commissioning switch armed, from STOP alone: it shows `STARTUP` with its reason and the phase `load`, and the next cycle ends the start-up and runs the program's first scan, the program from its initial values and its clock from zero, the plant handed the target's time; on a production target without real-time scheduling a start, on command or after a power cycle, leaves `STOP realtime_refused`. A stop is free, from a person or from the program itself, from RUN or STARTUP, to `STOP command(source)`, the program ending at once. A scan longer than twice its period faults the whole controller, `FAULT watchdog(task)`; in FAULT the plant's exchange goes on with the program's safe outputs, a stop and a start are refused, and only a person's acknowledge with the switch armed leaves it, to `STOP acknowledged`, from where a start runs the program again from its initial values. Every refusal says why in its text (the program may only stop, the switch is not armed, the command does nothing in this state, a production target without real time) and changes nothing, but a start refused for want of real time, which leaves `STOP realtime_refused`. While no program runs, stopped or faulted before any program ran, the controller reads the inputs and writes the program's safe outputs on the periods of the target's clock alone and at most once in each, the first after a stop or an acknowledge at the first period not before the next cycle and after the last exchange, the program's or its own. The conditions `sim` and `no_realtime` say how it runs, `overruns` counts the time errors since the start, and the health is core's. Where the plan is silent the readings are `docs/questions.md` Q228's.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-controlador/tests/states.rs | on virtual time, against core's own writer: a zero period refused when the controller comes up; a new controller in `STOP`, its health a function check, three exchanges of the safe outputs at 0, 10 and 20 ms; a cold start arriving at 13 ms shown as `STARTUP cold(id=202) load()`, then `RUN` with health normal, the program's scans at 13, 23 and 33 ms on the target's clock and at 0, 10 and 20 ms on its own; a start refused from the program, with the switch off and from STARTUP, each by its text, the state unchanged; a stop with no switch to `STOP command(id=101, source="oficina")`, the safe outputs from the next period, never twice at one instant, a stop in STOP refused, a stop from STARTUP and one from RUN by the program; ## C-131: The envelope and the MAC address: Ethernet II with one VLAN tag, the three spellings of a MAC
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the PROFINET codecs; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::ether` reads and writes Ethernet II with at most one 802.1Q tag: destination, source, the tag's priority, drop-eligible bit and VLAN id, the EtherType, and the payload with whatever pad followed it (each protocol above bounds itself). A frame is written padded to 60 bytes; a payload past 1500 bytes, a priority past 7 or a VLAN id past 4095 is refused, never cut; a frame shorter than 14 bytes (18 tagged) is an error naming its length. `profinet::mac::Mac` reads exactly the spellings `aa:bb:cc:dd:ee:ff`, `aa-bb-cc-dd-ee-ff` and `aabb.ccdd.eeff`, in either case, and refuses every other text with the text it got; it prints the first spelling in lower case, serialises as it prints, and tells a group address (multicast or broadcast) and a locally administered one. Wireshark's dissectors read every frame it writes as it was written.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/envelope.rs | the three spellings in both cases, twenty other texts refused (short, long, bare, mixed separators, short and long groups, a sign, a space, a separator at an end, the dotted form with six groups and the coloned with three, non-ASCII digits and letters), the message; the group, broadcast and local bits, the DCP and LLDP group addresses; serde both ways; an untagged frame and a tagged one to the byte (priority 6 with VLAN 0, every bit set, the drop bit alone), the pad to 60 and none past it; 1500 bytes taken and 1501 refused, priority 8 and VLAN 4096 refused; 13, 16 and 17 bytes refused and 18 taken; tshark reading source, destination, type, priority, drop bit, VLAN id and length of three frames with no complaint
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-131.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-132: DCP: identify, Hello, Get and Set as the standard writes them, a Set to one device only, damage kept with its reason
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope" and "Rules", the PROFINET codecs; Veredas 1's `dcp.rs`; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::dcp` reads and writes PROFINET DCP: Identify requests (every device, one station name, one alias) to the DCP group address with their response delay; Identify answers and Hellos with every identity block (MAC, IP suite with its BlockInfo of set and conflict, device vendor, NameOfStation, vendor and device id, role, options, alias, instance, OEM id, device initiative); Get requests as bare option and suboption pairs, and their answers; Set requests of a name, an IP suite and the identification signal with their BlockQualifier (permanent or temporary); and Set answers as one three-byte control response per block. Each block carries a BlockInfo or qualifier exactly where the standard puts one, and an odd block its pad. A Set to a group address, and a station name that breaks the standard's rules (1 to 240 characters, labels of 1 to 63 of `a`-`z`, digits and `-`, no hyphen at a label's edge, no port name, no address form), are refused before anything is written, the name never rewritten; a block past its 16-bit length or a PDU past what a frame carries is refused. Decoding is total: a block that does not fit its kind (its size, a missing info word, text that is not UTF-8) is kept with its bytes and the reason and the next block still decodes; a block running past the data stops decoding there, kept; bytes that make no block are kept; a damaged header is an error naming it; any bytes decode without a panic, and a decoded PDU writes back to itself (a block that ran past the data is written as the whole block it kept). Wireshark's dissectors read every frame it writes field for field, and tell apart Veredas 1's five-byte control response, which this codec reads as damaged.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/dcp.rs | the three Identify requests to the byte (an odd name padded) and read back with no BlockInfo; an answer with every field read back whole, block by block with its info, re-encoded to its own bytes, the smallest answer to the byte, an empty identity and a lone vendor id saying nothing; a Hello's IP BlockInfo for the six states of set and conflict both ways, a missing status read as set; a Get request to the byte, an odd last byte kept and written back, a Get answer with a name and a control response to the byte; Set requests of a name (permanent and temporary), an IP suite and the signal to the byte; four group targets refused for each Set, the message, a bad name refused first; thirty-one names against the rules, accepted and refused with the exact error, 240 and 241 characters, 63- and 64-character labels; a Set answer of two control responses to the byte, the error words; seventeen damaged blocks each kept with its exact reason beside a good next block, an overrun stopping with what came before kept, a short tail kept, the frame's pad left out; header damage named; a vendor text past the frame, a block past 65535 and a PDU past 65535 refused; twenty thousand noisy PDUs cut at every length decoding without a panic and writing back to themselves, those with an overrun settling after one rewrite; tshark reading ten frames' services, types, transaction ids, delays, filters, every identity field, the IP BlockInfo and its conflict bit, the qualifiers, the signal and the control responses' errors with no complaint, and reading the five-byte control response's error as 2
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-132.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-133: LLDP with PROFINET's, 802.1's and 802.3's TLVs: every TLV kept, what a TLV cannot carry refused
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope" and "Rules", the PROFINET codecs; Veredas 1's `lldp.rs`; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::lldp` reads and writes LLDP: chassis id, port id and time to live (mandatory: one missing is an error naming it), port description, system name, description and capabilities, management addresses with their OID, PROFINET's measured delays, port status, MRP port status (the ring's domain and MRRT's status: off, configured, up) and chassis MAC, IEEE 802.1's port VLAN id and IEEE 802.3's MAC/PHY status. A TLV it does not read, one that repeats what the PDU already said, and one that does not fit its type (text that is not UTF-8 among them) is kept with its bytes and written back, so a decoded PDU writes back to itself; a TLV running past the PDU is an error naming it; the End TLV, or the last whole TLV, ends the PDU; a value past 511 bytes, a type past 127, a management address outside 1 to 31 bytes, an OID past 255 bytes or a frame past 1500 is refused, never cut; a time to live of 0 says the sender is leaving; any bytes decode without a panic. Wireshark's dissectors read every frame it writes field for field.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/lldp.rs | the smallest PDU to the byte, in a padded frame to the LLDP group address; a PROFINET port with every TLV read back whole, its MRP and chassis MAC TLVs to the byte, the MRRT names, the capability names, the short port name and chassis key; a switch's port with a MAC chassis, a name port, an unknown TLV, two unread organisation TLVs and a management address with an OID kept and written back; a second chassis id, time to live, system name and chassis MAC, text that is not UTF-8, a short capabilities TLV, a bad management address and a short organisation TLV each kept unread with its bytes, ids that are not UTF-8 kept under their subtype; a short PDU, two overruns and four missing mandatory TLVs named, a TLV past the End ignored and a PDU with no End read; 511 bytes taken and 512 refused, a long chassis id, type 128, management addresses of 0, 1, 31 and 32 bytes, a 256-byte OID and a 1520-byte frame refused; a leaving sender; twenty thousand noisy PDUs cut at every length decoding without a panic and writing back to themselves; tshark reading three frames' every TLV field (subtypes, ids, time to live, descriptions, capabilities, the management address, the five delays, both port status words, the MRP domain and MRRT status, the chassis MAC, the port VLAN, the MAC/PHY fields, the TLV types in order) with no complaint
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-133.md
- **Status:** pending verification

- **Description:** A test file (`veredas-test`, version 1) and a function file (`veredas-functions`, version 1) are read into the model of `veredas_comissionador::model` and checked whole (`check_test` with a reader for the files a test uses, `load_test` from disk relative to the test file's folder, `check_functions` for a function file alone), as `docs/test-schema.md` says: the stamp first, two TOML lines read by core's one header reader with the suite's texts, no other key in the header; then the body, the reading of a file stopping at its first error of form, named with its file, line and column; then every problem of the check named the same way. Read: settings with their defaults (both modes, a run's bound of ten minutes) and the rest of a line for `id`, `area`, `portal` and `use`; steps of one or more of eleven actions, `branch` and `stop` last, `on` routes after them; branch arms by comparison, range or value, `else` last; routes to a step, a counted retry or an end; the layout; functions with typed ports, a description from the comments above them, `reads` and `timeout`; statements of script boxes and functions; expressions by precedence, built-ins by arity, strings with their escapes and `{ }` expressions, durations in whole milliseconds; the language's own words never a name (`max` not among them). What a run does with any of it is the engine's (phases 2 and 3), not this clause's.

the wait's condition and timeout and its routed timeout; the call's inputs and its outputs on the line after it; the script's two assignments; the branch's comparison, range with its retry, and else; the report's values and its interpolated note in four parts; the two ends), its eight positions, the function's signature, description, reads, timeout and body; checked with the function file, the counts 8, 1, 1 and 3 as the reference's sentence says, a name that is no variable settled as plant data and a variable's as its row's column; from disk beside its function folder, with CRLF line ends alike, and a missing function file named at its `use` with the call it leaves unresolved; the header's five refusals by the suite's texts (no format, no version, another format, another version, the format decided first) and another key by its line, comments in the header, a function file's own format; a line broken inside parentheses and before an arrow, and not elsewhere; every literal of the reference read to its value, and eleven unreadable texts each by its reason and place; the settings' defaults and the rest of a line trimmed, commented and unquoted, and every refusal of a setting the reference lists, by text and place; a used file read beside the test, two functions of one name across files, an unreadable file, a path used twice, a used file's problem named by its path from the test's folder; every action but `call` (the worked example's) read into its kind and block, with the counts, and the refusals of an action's form; a step's several actions, a step named twice, actions after `branch`, after `stop` and after `on`, a stray line; routes of each kind and the refusals of a route, an outcome or an arm; the six comparisons, a range, a list of values and an expression as patterns; every statement kind in order, `else if` across a line, and the refusals inside a script box; a call's inputs in any order and fewer outputs taken, and the refusals of a call; an expression's shape by precedence, the built-ins with their arities and their refusals, a function called inside an expression; ## C-290: The release manifest: a set of component versions pinned at one commit, read as versioned TOML and checked against history
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "How releases work": "a manifest that pins a tested set of component versions"; P-011, its manifest); ingest (docs/entrega-plan.md "What a release is made of")
- **Description:** A suite release's manifest, `releases/<release>.toml`, is `veredas-release` version 1, its header read by core's one reader (another format, another version, either key absent, refused by name; a body that is no TOML refused) and every key checked: a misspelled key refused wherever it sits and every required key's absence named; `release` SemVer; `commit` a full id of 40 lowercase hexadecimal characters; `target` a triple without whitespace; `toolchain` stated; at least one `[[component]]`, each a suite crate's name (`veredas-<nome>` or `orcl-<nome>`, L2's rule) listed once, with a SemVer version and its executables each named once; `[tested]` stating `suite` and `together`, none empty or padded. Checked against the repository's history, git's objects at the commit and never a checkout: the commit there and an ancestor of HEAD; every member under `crates/` the commit's workspace lists named in the manifest at the version its own manifest states there, shipping exactly the executables cargo builds for it (its `[[bin]]` tables, `src/main.rs` named after the package, `src/bin/` files and folders, the last two unless `autobins = false`); nothing else named; a later commit moving nothing. Across a releases folder, every file is a manifest named after its own release, and a component's version pinned by two releases names one tree (its folder's tree equal at both commits). `entrega check` runs all of it on a repository's releases (exit 0 with a line per release, 2 with every refusal, 1 on a usage error), and every manifest of this repository checks against its own history.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/entrega/tests/manifest.rs | the plan's worked example read key by key; the header's four refusals and a body that is no TOML; a key misspelled at the top, in a component and in `[tested]`, and an unknown key in `[tested]`; every required key missing (`commit`, `target`, `toolchain`, `[tested]`, `suite`); `release` not SemVer; four malformed commits (short, upper case, a letter past f, 41 characters); `target` empty and with a space; `toolchain` blank and padded; `[tested]` empty and padded; no component; seven names that are no suite crate and an `orcl-*` one accepted; a component twice; a version not SemVer; four executables' names refused and one listed twice; against throwaway repositories (a library, an executable by `src/main.rs`, one by `[[bin]]` and one by `src/bin/`, a tool beside them that is no component): a matching manifest checked, and still after a later commit moves a version; a component left out and one too many; a version that differs; an executable not shipped and one not built; `autobins = false`; a `src/bin/<folder>/main.rs`; a commit absent and one on a side branch; two releases at one tree and a third at changed code; a releases folder absent, a file named after another release and a file that is no manifest; the executable's line, its refusal and its usage; every manifest of this repository against its own history

## C-291: The cut: a release's executables built on the tester machine's distribution, packed into a bundle with its sums and run together on the plant it ships
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "How releases work": "shipped and installed together"; P-011, its recipe); ingest (docs/entrega-plan.md "How a release is cut")
- **Description:** `entrega cut <release>` cuts HEAD of a clean tree, a tree that differs from HEAD and a release whose manifest exists refused by name; a real cut runs the whole suite there first, behind the machine's lock, and refuses a red one, where a trial cut (`--trial`) skips it and its manifest says `not run: a trial cut`. It reads the components, their versions and executables and the recipe (`tools/entrega/maquina/maquina.toml`, `veredas-machine` v1, read through core's header reader, every key checked) at the commit; builds every executable with `cargo build --release --locked --offline` inside a builder machine of the tester machine's distribution (Debian 13 under QEMU with KVM, eight processors), from the commit's `git archive` and the `cargo vendor` of its lock file, under the development machine's `rustc` version installed from rustup's official source in a builder disk cached per toolchain; refuses, by name, an executable whose glibc, libstdc++ or C++ ABI versions (read by `objdump -p`) pass what the recipe says the distribution offers; packs the bundle from git's objects at the commit (the README, the suite's plan, every file reference, the shipped components' plans, the design references, the plant `tools/entrega/planta/`) with the executables, the manifest and `SHA256SUMS` over every other file, in name order, owned by root, dated at the commit, so the same commit and executables pack to the same bytes; extracts it again and refuses a file that does not match its sum; runs the bundle's bench and supervisão together on a copy of its plant whose ports alone change, and refuses unless every object of the loop is live, both sources are live, both parcels move along the loop, the write the binding offers is refused by portal's switch (403, "writes are turned off for this project"), and both executables exit 0 once their stdin closes; only then writes the manifest, which checks against the repository (C-290). Limits: a red suite's refusal is read from the code, not shown by a test; the run is on the development machine, the tester machine's own run is C-292.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/entrega/tests/bundle.rs, tools/entrega/tests/cut.rs | without a machine: an executable's needs read from `objdump -p` (the newest of each library's versions, GLIBC_2.44 refused against Debian 13's 2.41, a newer libstdc++ and C++ ABI refused, the bench's C-only needs); `SHA256SUMS` written byte for byte and a changed, an extra and a missing file each named; the manifest a cut writes read back field for field, a trial's statement; the recipe read and refused by key (another format, a misspelled key, an address not https, a digest in capitals, a package name with a space, a login in capitals, a password with a quote); the cut refusing a release already cut and a clone whose tree differs from HEAD; for real, with the feature `corte` (`cargo nextest run -p entrega --features corte --test cut`): a trial cut of HEAD built in the builder machine, its manifest naming HEAD and every component at HEAD's version, checking against the repository; the bundle's files (every executable in `bin/` and nothing else there, the docs and the plant, no source), its manifest byte for byte, its sums, a tampered copy refused, the plant as git holds it; every executable's needs within Debian 13's, portal's and supervisão's asking for the C++ library; packed again to the same bytes; the run's page, every object live, both parcels moving within the loop, the sorter's rate in its range, the write refused with portal's own text, the switch off in the feed, both exits 0 and each executable's own lines
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-292: The tester machine: a release's bundle provisioned into an OVA by the recipe, its shipped disk booting the plant on VMware's devices
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the architect, 2026-11-22: "a vmware VM running a light linux distro containing the whole release suite, used by testers and designers"; P-022); ingest (docs/entrega-plan.md "Part two: the tester machine")
- **Description:** `entrega maquina <release>` builds the tester machine of a release already cut, from its bundle and from the recipe and guide at the commit its manifest names, without sudo, VMware, packer or docker: the distribution's cloud image (Debian 13, a dated build) downloaded once into a cache outside the repository and refused unless it matches the recipe's pinned SHA-512; a copy-on-write disk of the recipe's size booted under QEMU with KVM with a NoCloud seed and the bundle on a data disk; the provisioning unattended (the recipe's packages from the distribution's mirror: Xfce under LightDM, Chromium, open-vm-tools, NetworkManager; the SSH server removed; the recipe's user; the release under `/opt/veredas/` with `current`, the executables on the `PATH`; the plant in the user's home; the bench and supervisão as services of the user from boot, a check of the release's files against `SHA256SUMS` at every boot; automatic login with the browser on the plant; the network handed to NetworkManager; cloud-init disabled, the machine's identity blanked, the free space trimmed), refused unless its log's last line and exit say it finished; the disk flattened into a stream-optimized VMDK; an OVF descriptor (the recipe's virtual hardware version, a paravirtual SCSI controller, a VMXNET3 card, BIOS firmware, the recipe's processors and memory, the disk's file, size and capacity) and a manifest of SHA-256 sums, tarred in that order as ustar into `veredas-<release>.ova`. The disk taken out of the OVA, booted copy-on-write under QEMU on VMware's paravirtual SCSI and VMXNET3 devices with a card address the provisioning never saw, checks its release's files at boot, serves the plant from the first boot to the host (every object live, both sources live, the parcels moving, the offered write refused by portal's switch), draws its desktop, and leaves the shipped bytes unchanged. Remainder, what only VMware proves: the OVA imported into Workstation Pro 17, Fusion 13 (Intel) and ESXi 7 and 8, VMware's own devices, its SVGA display with the `vmwgfx` driver and the guest tools; QEMU's `vmware-svga` is refused by that driver and the proof boot draws on QEMU's standard VGA.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/entrega/tests/maquina.rs | with the feature `maquina` (`cargo nextest run -p entrega --features maquina --test maquina`): a trial cut of HEAD run together, then the machine built by the recipe from its bundle; the OVA's members in order (descriptor, manifest, disk), each owned by root; the disk stream-optimized at the recipe's capacity; the descriptor well-formed XML naming the disk's file and size, its capacity, the stream-optimized format, the hardware version, the paravirtual SCSI controller, the VMXNET3 card, the memory and BIOS firmware; the manifest's two sums; the disk out of the OVA booted copy-on-write on `pvscsi` and `vmxnet3` with VMware's address prefix: the boot's check of the release's sums on the console, the guest naming both drivers, the plant from the host (the page, every object live, both sources live, both parcels moving, the write refused with portal's text), a screen dump of many colours, the disk's SHA-256 unchanged | manual: the OVA opened in VMware (the remainder above), the architect's to try
- **Status:** pending verification

Before the code, 2026-11-22: the language's shared plan (docs/orcl/README.md, law L6, with ORCL's rules carried from Veredas 1 in docs/orcl/language/), the execution model proposed and not ruled (docs/controlador/execution-model.md), the study of the interpreter's front end (docs/orcl/front-end.md), the Linux study (docs/controlador/linux-study.md) and the names of controlador's files (docs/controlador/files.md).
