### P-014: The suite's security round — requests a page of another site makes
- **Date:** 2026-11-16
- **Source:** user (docs/questions.md Q29: "Leave it for the security round", and for portal on its own "With the security round"; the architect's call for a suite-wide security round)
- **Description:** The open items the architect left to the suite's security round, nothing built before it. Neither supervisão's host nor portal's server reads a request's `Host` or `Origin`, so a name re-pointed at the address (DNS rebinding) is the page's own origin to a browser, whose script reads every session's data and presses an armed write; and portal's write and send routes parse a body as JSON whatever its `Content-Type`, so a page of any site posts a write as `text/plain` while writes are armed, the site never asked first (read from the code, not yet shown by a test). Both hold for portal nested in supervisão and for portal on its own. Exit: the round's decisions, each item a C-clause proven by a test that sends the request and sees it refused, or a limit the architect states; C-049's limit then points at that clause. Amended 2026-11-19: the round's wider list, with the architect's positions going in, is docs/questions.md Q43 (certificates past their end date and the end date after 2038, a signature over a download's manifest and a keyed check on requests that alter a controller, the review's entries 32 to 39).
- **Status:** pending

### P-017: The suite's robustness round
- **Date:** 2026-11-19
- **Source:** user (the architect's call of 2026-11-16 for "a whole new spec definition round: robustness (overall, concerning all the suite's critical conponents)"); ingest (docs/robustness.md)
- **Description:** What a machine built on the suite needs to run unattended for decades, as ruled so far: every request that alters a controller is robust and a download carries a hash; what must survive is the data RUN needs; the program stored twice with a hash per block, rechecked at power-on and on a schedule; a cycle's data permanent only after a successful cycle; 64-bit targets only; the date unconfirmed until NTP or the clock chip confirms it, time-of-day blocks waiting, the clock battery's and NTP's maintenance requests by the architect's table; certificates ending on the last 32-bit date of 2038 with a request a year before; a maximum and a warning cycle time; retained variables by what the target can do (every cycle, at power loss, or the last save flagged), a write from outside saved at once; the embedded portal's store a window in memory (built under P-019). It rules integrity; the authenticity of the same downloads and requests, a signature and a keyed check, is the security round's (P-014). The round is paused: five areas are not opened and the study of Linux for real time and recovery is owed (docs/questions.md Q36 to Q42).
- **Proposed verification:** the round finished with the architect, then each ruling a C-clause of the component it binds (controlador first, portal where embedded), proven by injecting the fault on the bench, or a limit the architect states.

### P-019: veredas-portal — the portal embedded in a controller and in Oficina
- **Date:** 2026-11-19
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 19 November 2026; docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026, not built"); ingest (docs/portal-plan.md)
- **Description:** Controlador embeds the full portal, with its store and its server, which reads controlador's memory asynchronously; Oficina embeds a portal too, and two portals speak portal's own HTTP interface (HTTPS later) on the controller's loopback behind SSH, forcing, breakpoints, downloads, start, stop and acknowledge carried on the same link; the embedded portal serves OPC UA and Modbus/TCP to other systems (the state, the faults, the variables the program marks), its master switch and whitelist the one gate for writes from outside, a protocol bound only when the project names the interface, OPC UA writes on a signed and encrypted channel with a user, Modbus writes from listed client addresses; controller state changes and faults stored like any other data; on a controller the store a fixed window in memory by default, recording to the drive the project's choice, the window written to the drive at a fault; the grep engine over logs at rest. C-034's wording of the light build as "controlador's embedding" is overtaken (the light build's proof stands, its consumer does not); that the store has no upper bound today is read from the code (docs/robustness.md). What else it meets: C-044's write surface is "a whitelist over the handles' own gates", "never a passthrough", so whether the commands carried for Oficina pass portal's own switch, the commissioning switch alone, or both is to be ruled (Q37); the OPC UA server that exists (C-026: anonymous, or one user by name and password) has no signed or encrypted mode, which is new work; reading controlador's memory is a source beside C-043's three protocols; C-046's import names an in-memory store's limit and the grep as not carried, which changes once they are built; the store's window is the ruling P-017 states, built here. Open: docs/questions.md Q31 (entries 22 and 33), Q37, Q41, Q43.
- **Proposed verification:** each a C-clause of portal proven against the bench, C-034 reworded with the first of them.
